| `database` (+ `ffldb`) | `dcroxide-database` | semantics | The `database/v3 v3.0.3` interface semantics over redb 4.3.0 per accepted [ADR-0004](docs/adr/0004-storage-backend.md): all 22 error kinds with dcrd's names plus a 23rd, `Fatal`/`ErrFatal`, that dcrd has no counterpart for (the fail-closed latch, in the divergence table below), transactions (managed `view`/`update` with dcrd's panic-on-commit/rollback semantics, unmanaged `begin`, single-writer/multi-reader, snapshot isolation, read-your-writes), buckets and cursors over ffldb's exact key layout (4-byte bucket IDs, `bidx` bucket index rows, `bidx-cbid` counter) so iteration order and nested-bucket behaviors match ffldb observably, plus a windowed cursor ffldb has no counterpart for: ffldb's is a pair of lazy merged iterators, so dcrd's batched index drop can restart it per batch for free, while this one materializes the keys it walks and restarting it once re-read the whole bucket (66,494,886 rows for mainnet's `existsaddridx`) — every scan, windowed or not, is one merge join of the store range, the overlay's merged layers and the transaction's pending puts, with its pending deletions as a mask, pulled only as far as the window reaches, so a window costs O(limit) wherever its keys live. `Bucket::for_each` streams in 4,096-row windows and reads each value once, and `Cursor::delete` parks the deleted value lazily rather than reading it (ffldb's `Delete` reads nothing). A store read error ends the store's side of a scan while the overlay and pending rows carry on, as ffldb's merged cursor treats a goleveldb iterator error as exhaustion and `ForEach` never checks it. redb's range iterator instead repeats `PreviousIo` forever, so a scan that skipped errors had spun. Also ported: flat `*.fdb` block files in dcrd's exact record byte format (LE network ‖ LE length ‖ block ‖ CRC-32C), block index rows as location ‖ header with headers always served from metadata, pending-block visibility within a transaction, and `reconcileDB` recovery on both `open` and `create`, as dcrd runs it for both: block-file bytes past the stored write cursor are rolled back, so `create` over leftover `*.fdb` files deletes all but the first and truncates it, as dcrd's create-time reconcile does. Block data short of the cursor is `ErrCorruption` with dcrd's text, and a store with no cursor is `ErrCorruption` "write cursor does not exist". It also ports `handleRollback`'s reposition of the write cursor whatever fails, and `writeData`'s advance of the cursor by the bytes a failed write actually wrote. dcrd's BCDB lines for all of it (`Detected unclean shutdown - Repairing...`, `Database sync complete`, `***Database corruption detected***`, and the `ROLLBACK:` debug line and warnings) go through `Options::log`. The daemon points it at `BCDB` as dcrd's `log.go:88` does, and addblock does the same as dcrd's `cmd/addblock/addblock.go:76` does (`dcroxide_node::logging::bcdb_log_sink`). `create` refuses with `ErrDbExists` a directory that holds dcrd's own `metadata/` leveldb store: dcrd's create refuses its own existing metadata store (`ErrorIfExist`), and both daemons name the directory `blocks_ffldb`, so without the check a dcroxide pointed at a dcrd data directory would roll back and delete dcrd's block files. Open-time failures that redb reports as corruption (header validation, a repair that finds every root corrupted, an aborted repair) are `ErrCorruption` as `convertErr` would make them. A file that is not a redb database at all stays `ErrDriverSpecific`. A data directory on an older redb file format is refused on open with an upgrade message rather than misread: redb 4 reads only file format 3 and there is no in-place migration, so such a directory has to be re-synced (the chain is not damaged). No directory in an older format exists to refuse — dcroxide has never been released — so the message is held by its own wording and not by a test. Parity instrument: the ported ffldb interface-test battery plus a 12-test crash-consistency rig (torn-write and missing-data tears, randomized tear soak, cross-bucket atomicity over the multi-bucket commit `Chain::flush` makes, both desync directions of its state markers, a kill between the block-file sync and the metadata commit, and deletes torn from the puts beside them) and an ENOSPC fault-injection test that fills a 2 MiB filesystem under a live database (Linux-only, and CI sets `DCROXIDE_REQUIRE_FAULT_INJECTION=1` so a skip is a failure) — a live Go oracle is the wrong tool for stateful storage. Durability is asserted rather than inherited: every write transaction is opened by one helper that sets `Durability::Immediate`, and a source-scanning test fails on any other `begin_write` call site (`tests/durability_policy.rs`). Documented divergences, all within the interface contract: the metadata write cache is ported (dcrd ffldb's `dbCache`: transaction-local pending key/remove sets, a shared in-memory overlay committed to redb in one durable transaction only at flush — 100 MiB / 300 s thresholds with dcrd's 1.5x `needsFlush` rule — with the flat block files synced *before* the metadata commit so metadata can never describe unsynced bytes, and flushes on `Flush`, `Close`, and the chain's clean-shutdown flush; the overlay is a stack of immutable layers where dcrd snapshots an immutable treap, so a transaction's snapshot is an O(1) clone of the layer handles and a commit writes into the newest layer in place unless a reader has frozen it, with the size accounting counting the bytes the layers retain rather than the overlay's logical size; commit is accordingly durable only at flush points, dcrd's own cache contract, and an unclean shutdown loses only the cached window — pinned by the crash rig's lazy-window test), no LRU file-handle cache, snapshot cursors, and `Cursor::delete` on a read-only tx returns the contract's `ErrTxNotWritable` where ffldb silently discards it; `Flush` and `Close` wait out the writer but not open read-only transactions, where dcrd's take `closeLock` exclusively and wait for readers too (`ffldb/db.go:1969-1971`, `:2010-2012`). A reader here holds its own overlay layers and redb snapshot, which a flush neither changes nor frees, so the wait would buy nothing, and a long-lived reader does not hold up a flush or a close. ffldb quirks reproduced: empty-key `delete` silently succeeds; a bucket and a plain key may share a name. The `addblock` bootstrap format (LE net ‖ LE len ‖ block): `bootstrap::read_block` is the one port of dcrd's `readBlock`. It uses Go's `EOF`/`unexpected EOF` texts and dcrd's mismatch and payload-cap texts, treats a trailing fragment as an error, and retries an interrupted read. Two callers read with it: the daemon's `addblock`, which runs every block through the chain as dcrd's importer does, and the bench. Export is byte-identical. `Database::import_blocks` is a storage-test helper with no production caller. It stores blocks with no block index, UTXO or consensus work and is not an importer. Since ported: the compressed UTXO encodings (with the blockchain serialization piece).  Documented architectural divergence: dcrd v1.10 keeps the UTXO set in its own separate database (`LoadUtxoDB` over `utxobackend.go`'s leveldb backend); the port persists the UTXO set, its set-state marker, and the best state in the block database, with the entry writes and the marker coupled in one transaction everywhere the cache flushes (dcrd's backend `PutUtxos` coupling).  When a block database backs it, the chain keeps only a recent window of block bodies, spend journals, GCS filters, header commitments, per-height ticket rows and treasury state rows in memory; without one the mirrors are the only copy and are never evicted. Every one is written through to the block database per connect and read back through database fallbacks when absent. They are evicted below `minMemoryStakeNodes` (288) of the tip by a port of dcrd's `chainPruner` (time-gated on the target block interval, called from `ProcessBlock`; the stake-node clearing matches `pruneStakeNodes`). They are also evicted for each connected block's parent as soon as that block is more than 288 below the best header, which is when dcrd's `connectBlock` drops the parent's stake node (`chain.go:795-808`). This keeps the window flat during initial sync instead of letting it grow for a whole prune interval. A treasury row read back follows dcrd's handling of `dbFetchTreasuryBalance`: a missing row ends `sumPastTreasuryChanges`' walk; a row that does not decode is an error that fails the expenditure check as `ErrInvalidExpenditure`; `calculateTreasuryBalance` reads either as a zero balance. A restart warms only the window. dcrd keeps none of these mirrors: its treasury rows, in particular, are read per lookup, where the port once loaded the whole treasury bucket; and the UTXO set itself lives only on disk behind a full port of dcrd's `UtxoCache`: a read-through cache (backend misses are cached, absent outputs negatively cached, fetched clones handed out with the modified/fresh flags cleared) and write-back cache with dcrd's fresh/modified/spent entry states, dcrd's exact size accounting (mapOverhead 57 + outpoint 56 + pointer 8 per entry plus baseEntrySize 56 + script length), height-based eviction of 15% of the depth since the last eviction once the configured `--utxocachemaxsize` is reached, and dcrd's full trigger set — size, the two-minute periodic interval during the initial sync, a forced flush per connect once the chain believes it is current, per disconnect, on the is-current latch flipping after a reorganization, and before `FetchStats` folds the set.  `UtxoCache.Initialize`'s catch-up replay runs at every open: a fresh backend records its state at the tip, a lagging state disconnects blocks to the fork point through their spend journals and replays forward through the cache to the tip — so an unclean shutdown mid-sync recovers instead of silently serving a stale set.  As in dcrd, the replay checks the shutdown interrupt before every block it detaches or replays, and it stops with `errInterruptRequested` ("interrupt requested"). The daemon passes its interrupt flag to `Chain::open_with_interrupt` and logs a failed chain open the way dcrd logs `newServer`'s error (`dcrd.go:243`), so an interrupted start reads "Unable to start server: interrupt requested". The unflushed tail is replayed again at the next start, as in dcrd.  Pinned by the persistence vectors' restart round trips, the prune test replaying dcrd's full block battery over a one-byte cache limit, DB-path stats tests over seeded utxo rows, and a crash-recovery test replaying the battery under an initial-sync clock, dropping the chain with the recorded state mid-chain, and verifying the reopened set folds to the byte-exact stats (serialized merkle hash included) of a cleanly flushed run.  Retained divergences: one database rather than dcrd's separate leveldb `utxodb` (so no separate `UtxoBackendInfo` versioning or upgrade path — the block database's own versioning covers the combined store, and the single-transaction flush coupling is strictly stronger than dcrd's cross-database flush ordering); the periodic flush interval runs off the adjusted-time clock threaded through `ProcessBlock` rather than the wall clock; the open-time catch-up replay runs at the default cache size before the configured `--utxocachemaxsize` is applied by the daemon; utxo backend corruption found by a point read panics (the resolver seam has no error channel) where dcrd propagates an error, and so does a redb *read fault* on that seam. UTXO rows and the UTXO set state are read with `Bucket::try_get`, which returns the error, as dcrd's `levelDbUtxoBackend.Get` separates `ErrNotFound` from a real error and `dbFetchUtxoEntry`/`FetchState` propagate it. The `gettxoutsetinfo` walk uses `Bucket::try_for_each`, which fails the way `FetchStats` does on `iter.Error()` (pinned by `dcroxide-blockchain/tests/review_utxo_read_faults.rs`). Every other ffldb-layout read keeps ffldb's `dbCacheSnapshot.Get` answer of absence for an error, and a `begin` whose metadata table cannot be opened fails rather than reading every key as missing. Error-as-absence on the UTXO set was not bounded to one transaction: after an I/O error redb keeps serving cached pages and fails every uncached one with `PreviousIo` until restart; the double-spend and zero-conf-spend sanity checks in the commit path assert where dcrd returns `AssertError`, for the same want of an error channel; and the flush hit-ratio debug logging is not reproduced |
| `internal/blockchain` | `dcroxide-blockchain` | vectors | The UTXO serialization layer from `compress.go`, `utxoentry.go`, `utxoio.go`, and `chainio.go`'s minimal-outputs size reader: VLQs (MSB base-128 with the offset subtraction), the domain-specific script compression (six special forms incl. uncompressed-pubkey point recovery via dcroxide-dcrec; unrecognized scripts behind VLQ(len+64)), the base-10 exponent amount compression, compressed txout encode/decode with dcrd's error text, txout/entry flag bytes (raw stake-type bits survive round trips like dcrd's unchecked cast), `UtxoEntry` with its state/flag accessors, outpoint keys (prefix [3,3] ‖ hash ‖ VLQ tree ‖ VLQ index), entry serialization incl. the ticket-submission minimal-outputs tail, and the UTXO set state row. dcrd keeps these in an internal package the oracle cannot import, so parity is pinned by dcrd's own test tables extracted mechanically from the Go test sources (57 vectors) plus randomized round-trip properties. Quirk noted: dcrd's `putCompressedScript` opens with a dead always-panic `len(target) == 0` branch; unreachable via any caller and not reproduced. Corrupt compressed scripts diverge. An overlong size VLQ can wrap to a special discriminant, or to a general size shorter than the VLQ, and then claim fewer bytes than its payload. dcrd's `decompressScript` slices past the script into the backing array or panics. The port's `decode_compressed_tx_out` returns a deserialize error, which the chain database reports as corruption. The node never writes such an encoding. Also ported: the legacy difficulty algorithms from `difficulty.go` — the exponentially-weighted BLAKE-256 work retarget (64.32 fixed point, genesis-parking window walks, testnet reduce-min-difficulty and max-difficulty rules), the BLAKE3 (DCP0011) difficulty from an anchor node, `mergeDifficulty`, `estimateSupply`, and both stake difficulty algorithms (original and DCP0001), with the chain walk abstracted behind a height-indexed `ChainView`. Parity: 35 synthetic-chain scenarios generated inside dcrd's own package with dcrd's outputs frozen and replayed (three networks, retarget-boundary lengths), plus pure-function samples. Also ported: the stake version machinery from `stakeversion.go` plus `calcWantHeight` and the 11-block past-median-time (even-count quirk preserved) — interval-final node location, header stake version majorities, prior stake version, voter version interval walks, block version majorities, and `calcStakeVersion` including its no-return nil-ancestor fall-through; pinned by 18 synthetic simnet chains generated inside dcrd's package plus 60 `calcWantHeight` samples, all replaying exactly (dcrd's four per-hash memoization caches are reproduced on the block index's node store through the view's `cache_*` hooks, keyed as dcrd keys them. `calcStakeVersionCache` is keyed by the voter version node, and the stake majority and prior stake version caches by the prior interval's final node, so every block of an interval shares one entry and the interval walks run once per interval. They are result-invariant, and the vectors replay the uncached path). Also ported: the threshold state machine from `thresholdstate.go` (`nextThresholdState` with the full Defined→Started→LockedIn→Active/Failed transitions, vote tallying with choice-index masking, quorum and activation thresholds, deployment version lookups, and forced-choice resolution for test networks), pinned by 195 state-and-choice verdicts over 15 synthetic simnet chains covering every deployment, all replaying exactly; dcrd's per-deployment hash-keyed cache is reproduced through the view's threshold-state hooks. That includes the defined state dcrd caches at the boundary whose median time precedes the start time, and the boundaries below the stake validation height. The cache is result-invariant, and the vectors replay the uncached path. Also ported: the agenda activation helpers and algorithm selectors (`is*AgendaActive` for all ten vote IDs incl. the treasury genesis special case and the forced-choice fast path, the BLAKE3 work-difficulty anchor search, and the `calcNextRequiredDifficulty`/`calcNextRequiredStakeDifficulty` selectors), pinned by 156 verdicts over 12 synthetic simnet chains from dcrd's own package. Also ported: the chain persistence serialization formats from `chainio.go` — ticket minimal outputs, block index keys/entries (header ‖ status ‖ VLQ vote tuples), the spend journal (reverse-serialized compressed stxos with the vote-stakebase skip and ticket minimal-outputs tails), header commitments, and the best chain state (LE fields ‖ stripped big-endian work sum, incl. the all-zero-keeps-32-bytes quirk and SetByteSlice truncation on load) (the block index flush points and cadence follow dcrd's -- the load path, block connect and disconnect, every accepted header (`process.go:267-271`; like ffldb's, each flush is a commit into the database's metadata write cache, not a sync, which reaches disk only when the cache flushes), and the administrative invalidate/reconsider paths dcrd flushes warn-only) — pinned by 150 vectors from dcrd's own package with Go-side round trips proven before freezing. Also ported: all 160 consensus rule error kinds (machine-generated) and the context-free validation layer from `validate.go` — `CheckTransaction` (standalone sanity + the agenda-driven context checks across coinbase/vote/revocation/treasury shapes, script version and stake-opcode rules), `CheckProofOfStake`, and the dual-hash `checkProofOfWorkSanity` — pinned by 520 verdicts over structured and mutated transactions, blocks, and headers from dcrd's own package. Also ported: the block sanity layer — `checkBlockHeaderSanity` (PoW sanity, the +2h future-time limit against an adjusted-time parameter standing in for dcrd's `MedianTimeSource`, the pre-stake-validation-height commitments incl. the 0x0001 early vote bits and zero final state, and the vote/fresh-stake count limits; dcrd's sub-second timestamp precision check is unreachable here since wire timestamps are whole seconds by type) and `checkBlockSanity` (proof of stake, non-empty regular tree, `MaxBlockPayload` and header size commitment, per-tree standalone transaction sanity, the fresh-stake header commitment tally, and duplicate transaction detection) — pinned by 400 verdicts over boundary-biased headers and assembled blocks from dcrd's own package. Also ported: the sequence lock (relative lock time) calculation from `sequencelock.go` — `calcSequenceLock` (DCP0003: per-input disable/seconds/mask bit handling, mempool-height inputs viewed from the next block, block-based locks from input heights, time-based locks from the past median time of the block before the input's, and the coinbase/stakebase/version<2 exemptions incl. the internally derived treasury agenda state) and `LockTimeToSequence` — with dcrd's `UtxoViewpoint` lookup abstracted to a confirmation-height closure, which the engine fills from its utxo view and the mempool adapter from its own; pinned by 264 vectors over synthetic simnet chains (treasury forced-active there) and mainnet chains (inactive branch) from dcrd's own package. Also ported: the positional validation layer — `checkDifficultyPositional` (the forced-BLAKE3, EMA, and candidate-anchor-walk paths, incl. dcrd's signed-to-unsigned header version cast; both of dcrd's cached BLAKE3 anchors are reproduced on the block index store. The first is the confirmed anchor (`cachedBlake3WorkDiffAnchor`): every contextual BLAKE3 difficulty calculation records it, and it then holds descendant headers to the ASERT difficulty alone. That is a rule rather than an optimization, since a synced node rejects EMA-bits headers that a cold one accepts. The second is the candidate anchor (`cachedBlake3WorkDiffCandidateAnchor`), which is tried before the candidate walk. The vector views leave both empty and so replay a cold dcrd; `tests/review_blake3_anchor.rs` pins the warm verdicts), `checkBlockHeaderPositional` (past-median-time, the testnet3 minimum-time rule and the height-962928 max-diff checkpoint, height commitments, and old-version-by-majority rejection with the DCP0005 violation exemptions and all six pinned block hashes; the fork rejection checkpoint check landed with the headers-first processing layer once the block index arrived), `checkBlockDataPositional`/`checkBlockPositional` (expired transactions), and the pure helpers (`voteBitsApproveParent`, `IsExpiredTx`, `SequenceLockActive`, `IsFinalizedTransaction`) — pinned by 276 vectors over six scenario chains spanning all four networks, incl. a base-offset testnet3 chain around the max-diff activation height and regnet headers ground to solve BLAKE3 so the candidate anchor walk is exercised both succeeding and falling back. Found and fixed in the process: the difficulty code's testnet3 detection used the old testnet2 network magic (`0x48e7a065` instead of wire's `0xb194aa75`); the frozen difficulty vectors were unaffected (regenerated identical) but the max-difficulty clamp would never have applied on real testnet3 data. Also ported: the contextual header validation layer — `checkProofOfWorkContext` (hash algorithm selected by the DCP0011 agenda state), `checkBlockHeaderContext` (agenda-driven work and stake difficulty, the version-3 stake version majority enforcement, and — now that the ticket state machine is ported — the pool size and ticket lottery final state commitments against the caller-supplied parent stake node state), `checkMerkleRoots` (combined root under DCP0005 header commitments, dual roots otherwise), the `is{Blake3Pow,HeaderCommitments}AgendaActive` wrappers, and the coinbase/treasurybase unique-height checks (`checkCoinbaseUniqueHeight`/`checkTreasurybaseUniqueHeight` with the consensus-specialized OP_RETURN null-data parsing, incl. the 256-byte cap and 4-byte little-endian height) — pinned by 175 vectors over simnet (both agendas forced active), mainnet, and regnet chains with headers ground for the agenda-correct hash algorithm and crafted null-data scripts. Also ported: the stake transaction input validation layer — the stake-tagged P2PKH/P2SH script extractors, `isAllowedTicketInputScriptForm`/`isStakeSubmission`, the ticket commitment field extractors (hash/amount with the P2SH flag bit/fee limits), `checkTicketPurchaseInputs` (dcrd's `UtxoViewpoint` lookup abstracted to a `UtxoEntry`-returning closure), `calcTicketReturnAmounts` (64.32 fixed point over arbitrary precision with Go's lossy `Int64` narrowing, incl. the auto-revocation remainder distribution via Hash256PRNG seeded with the previous header), `checkTicketSubmissionInput`, `checkTicketRedeemerCommitments` (payee type/hash/value against the ticket commitments with the log2 fee limit semantics incl. the ≥63 full-fee rule and the treasury-vote output exclusion honoring Go's nil-slice `CheckSSGenVotes` semantics), and `checkVoteInputs`/`checkRevocationInputs` (stakebase subsidy commitment via the V3 subsidy cache with a `ChainSubsidyParams` adapter over the chain parameters, submission input/index/maturity rules with the +1/+2/auto-revocation variants) — pinned by 149 vectors over crafted tickets, votes, and revocations against a fabricated utxo view from dcrd's own package. Also ported: `CheckTransactionInputs` — the input validation orchestrator returning the transaction fee: coinbase/treasurybase early-outs, delegation to the ticket/vote/revocation input checks, treasury spend Pi-key validation (`verifyTSpendSignature` over the empty-script `SigHashAll` sighash with EC-Schnorr-DCRv0), the stakebase-subsidy and tspend value-in accumulation exceptions, zero-value spend bans, the fraud proof triple, coinbase/expiry/OP_TGEN/OP_SSGEN/OP_SSRTX/stake-change maturities (with the treasury-dependent stake output maturity), the OP_SSTX spend restriction, the unspendable treasury add rule, and the amount range/overflow/fee accounting — pinned by 88 vectors on simnet incl. treasury spends actually signed with the published simnet Pi test key, all replaying exactly. Also ported: the signature operation and stake amount accounting — `countSigOps` (imprecise per-script counting with the stakebase/treasurybase skips), `countP2SHSigOps` (precise redeem-script counting over the utxo lookup with the overflow guard), the exported `CountTotalSigOps` that sums the two, the cumulative enforcement against the 5,000 per-block limit, inline in `checkTransactionsAndConnect` (`validate.go:3841-3858`), `checkStakeBaseAmounts`/`getStakeBaseAmounts` (vote subsidy limits aligned to the height voted on), the stake tree's fees returned by `checkTransactionsAndConnect` and carried into the regular tree's total (the 2.2 campaign delta below records how both took this shape), and `checkDupTxs` (a no-op at this dcrd version per its disabled `checkForDuplicateHashes` constant, ported with the dormant logic) — pinned by 170 vectors over sigop-bearing P2SH scripts and vote sets from dcrd's own package. Also ported (beginning the Phase 8 chain engine proper): the in-memory block index and chain view from `blockindex.go`/`chainview.go` — block nodes with the deterministic skip-list ancestor traversal, accumulated work sums (via the pinned `CalcWork`), the stable status bit flags, header reconstruction, lottery IVs, past median times, and the `betterCandidate` ordering (work, then data availability, then received order, then the little-endian-uint256 hash tiebreak); the block index with chain tip tracking, best header/invalid selection, best chain candidates, unlinked-children data linking (`AcceptBlockData`), and `MarkBlockFailedValidation`'s descendant invalidation walk over the (cached) chain tips; and the height-indexed chain view with efficient tip switching, fork finding, and block locator construction (dense-then-doubling with `fastLog2Floor` sizing). Go's parent-pointer graph is an arena (`NodeStore` with index handles) shared by the index and views; dcrd's short-key/collision map pair (a memory optimization), mutex wrappers, and database flush machinery are documented as not reproduced; the cached-tips prune timer (`MaybePruneCachedTips`) lives in the engine's connect path and runs on the adjusted-time clock instead of the wall clock. Parity: dcrd's own blockindex_test.go and chainview_test.go batteries ported in full (header round trips, the median time table, chain tips, the 250,000-node skip list with 2,500 random probes, the ancestor relationship table, the complete betterCandidate table, three-branch view containment/next/fork/locator layouts, tip switching, and empty views), plus a supplementary invalidation-and-linking test for the paths dcrd covers via integration tests. Also ported (the chain engine scaffolding proper): the headers-first processing layer from `process.go` — a `Chain` state over the arena-backed block index carrying the assumed-valid and old-fork-rejection configuration (`expectedBlocksInTwoWeeks` derived from the target block time, fork rejection disabled when configured off or the network has no hard-coded assumed valid hash), `checkKnownInvalidBlock`, `maybeAcceptBlockHeader`/`ProcessBlockHeader` (known-status short circuit, optional header sanity, orphan and invalid-ancestor rejection, the positional checks, index insertion; `ProcessBlockHeader` then flushes the block index, as dcrd does after every accepted header), automatic fork rejection checkpoint discovery (`maybeSetForkRejectionCheckpoint`), assumed-valid node tracking (`maybeUpdateAssumeValid`), and `isAssumeValidAncestor` with its two-weeks-behind-best-header clamp — plus the `NodeBranchView` adapter that walks a block tree branch through the deterministic skip list to serve the height-indexed chain views every previously ported validation function consumes, and the completed `ErrForkTooOld` check inside `checkBlockHeaderPositional` (the fork rejection facts dcrd reads from its index mid-check are supplied by the processing layer; a `None` checkpoint skips the check, keeping the earlier frozen vectors byte-compatible). Pinned by 48 state-machine vectors from dcrd's own package: a 30-header simnet chain with ground proofs of work fed through the real `maybeAcceptBlockHeader` over a real block index, covering acceptance, duplicates, orphans, sanity failures honored and skipped, positional failures, side chains above and below the discovered checkpoint, a mid-chain invalidation with the known-invalid short circuits, and the clamped assumed-valid ancestry checks, comparing verdicts, node status bytes, the best header, and the tracked checkpoint and assumed-valid nodes at every step. Also ported: the stake node attachment layer from `stakenode.go` — `maybeFetchNewTickets` (the maturing tickets loaded by walking back the ticket maturity distance, with the `None`-versus-empty distinction preserved), `maybeFetchTicketInfo` (vote and revocation info populated from the block data via the already ported `FindSpentTicketsInBlock`; dcrd tracks population through slice nil-ness. The port's flag is cleared wherever dcrd leaves or sets the slices nil: the block index load and both stake-node pruners. The block is read through the database fallback, a missing body is an error rather than a panic (as in dcrd), and the node is marked modified as dcrd's `PopulateTicketInfo` marks it. `flushBlockIndex`'s reload uses dcrd's `canValidate` gate), and `fetchStakeNode` with all three of its paths: the cache hit, the parent connect, and the full regeneration walk that disconnects from the current best chain tip back to the fork point — restoring each pruned node's own bookkeeping from the per-height ticket database undo and tickets rows exactly as dcrd loads them inside the disconnect — and then replays any side chain blocks up to the requested node; the `Chain` state grew the best chain view, the block storage, and the per-height ticket rows (recent-window mirrors of what the database holds: the block store mirrors the blocks stored as their data is accepted, standing in for dcrd's recent block cache, and the per-height ticket rows mirror the `WriteConnectedBestNode` undo and new-tickets rows `connect_block` writes in its connect transaction; entries that leave the window are read back from the database, and a chain without a database keeps every entry, since the mirrors are then the only copy), and block nodes now carry the optional stake node and maturing tickets like dcrd's. Pinned by 215 vectors from dcrd's own package: a 154-block simnet chain of real skeleton blocks (ticket purchases maturing on schedule, votes with random misses, and revocations) advanced through the real `fetchStakeNode` with ticket database rows written at every height, a side chain forked below the tip, then 55 prunes followed by fetches that force the tip-to-fork disconnect walk over the undo rows, the side chain replay, the fork-point path, and the cache hit — comparing pool sizes, lottery final states, winner lists, and missed tickets. Also ported: the reorganization engine from `chain.go` — `BestState`/`newBestState` (incl. the genesis initial snapshot from `createChainState`), `connectBlock` (the block index flush; best state accumulation of transactions and added subsidy; the best state, spend journal record, ticket database rows, treasury balance and treasury spend rows, filter, and header commitment leaves written in one database transaction; the view commit and conditional UTXO cache flush; the tip move followed by the timed cached-tip prune (`MaybePruneCachedTips`, every 5 minutes on the adjusted-time clock, with the timestamp set by the first connect after start rather than by the load); the connected and new-tickets notifications; and the immediate prune of the parent's stake node. Only the difficulty retarget debug log lines and `addRecentBlock` are not reproduced; the recent-window `blocks` mirror stands in for dcrd's recent block cache and, like it, hands the connect path and the notifications one shared copy of each block), `disconnectBlock` (the parent snapshot with the disconnected block's own stake difficulty commitment, the ticket row drops above the new tip per `WriteDisconnectedBestNode`, and the journal record removal), `loadOrCreateFilter`, `reorganizeChainInternal` (the tip-to-fork detach loop over the spend journal and the attach loop with the validated fast path and the full `checkBlockContext` + `checkConnectBlock` path, which marks failed validation only for consensus rule violations (`RuleErrorKind::is_rule_violation`, dcrd's `errors.As(err, &RuleError)`). Local corruption such as an undecodable parent spend journal row fails the reorg without branding the block, and the recent-context-checks cache skips the context checks for a block that just passed them), `reorganizeChain` with the failed-reorg fallback to the best valid candidate, and the is-current latch (`isOldTimestamp`/`maybeUpdateIsCurrent`/`isCurrent` against a caller-supplied adjusted time).  Three pieces of dcrd's utxo cache semantics proved observable and are reproduced exactly over the in-memory backend+cache stand-ins: the spend journal serialization is round tripped because dcrd reconstructs spent entry amounts, heights, and indexes from the spending inputs' fraud proofs on load; fresh (never flushed) cache entries are removed outright on spend with an explicit spent marker while flushed entries become tombstones that disconnects restore in place with original fields; and the forced cache flush on every disconnect drops the remaining tombstones so blocks detached later in the same reorganization resurrect their outputs from the journal instead.  Pinned by 771 vectors generated against a complete real `BlockChain` (`New()` over real block and utxo databases): two competing branches of pre-validated skeleton blocks reorganized forward, across, back, and extended, comparing the best state snapshot and all 732 presence-and-field checks over the full utxo universe after every reorganization.  Also ported: the full block intake path completing `process.go` — `maybeAcceptBlockData` (prunable ticket info population, the positional data checks with failed-validation marking, block storage, and the index data acceptance producing the newly fully-linked descendants; the `chainPruner` call dcrd makes here runs from `ProcessBlock` once the call's reorganization is done, which changes only when the memory is released), `maybeAcceptBlocks` (contextual checks over each linked block, with first-error semantics, the recent-context-checks cache, and the early new-tip-checked notification for a block that extends the tip of a current chain. A failure is marked as failed validation only when it is a consensus rule violation, dcrd's `errors.As(err, &RuleError)` gate. `addRecentBlock` is not reproduced: the `blocks` mirror, which holds the block from its data acceptance, stands in for the recent block cache), and `ProcessBlock` itself (duplicate and known-invalid rejection, full block sanity, header acceptance reusing the headers-first path, the bulk-import/assumed-valid fast path that pre-marks validation, data acceptance, tentative acceptance of all linked blocks, reorganization to the best chain candidate regardless of acceptance failures exactly like dcrd, and the fork length result).  Pinned by 261 vectors generated against a complete real `BlockChain` driving the real `ProcessBlock`/`ProcessBlockHeader` over ground proof-of-work simnet blocks (the sanity checks accept either proof of work hash while the contextual checks demand the agenda-selected BLAKE3 hash, so the generator grinds for both): sequential intake, a duplicate, an orphan, a headers-first delivery with out-of-order data linking, a mid-processing reorganization to a longer branch, sanity and positional rejections, a full-validation connect failure with the known-invalid (reported as a duplicate per dcrd's check order) and invalid-ancestor short circuits, and a losing-branch extension — comparing verdicts, fork lengths, node status bytes, best state snapshots, and the final utxo universe.  Also ported: the manual chain manipulation surface — `InvalidateBlock` (the genesis and unknown block rejections, the already-failed no-op, the mark-only path for side chain blocks, and for best chain blocks the rollback to the parent, the descendant invalidation, the current-latch reset, the best chain candidate rescan over all chain tips walking each back to its first valid validatable ancestor, and the reorganization to the best remaining candidate), `ReconsiderBlock` (clearing invalidity up the ancestor chain while tracking the earliest failure, clearing the invalid ancestor flag across descendants that are not otherwise invalid, restoring candidates and unlinked children entries, recomputing the best invalid block and best header over all tips, and reorganizing), and `forceHeadReorganization`/`ForceHeadReorganization` (the same-block, wrong-chain, missing-child, known-invalid, and no-data rejections before reorganizing to a tip sibling).  Pinned by 37 operation vectors (plus the intake and 201 utxo universe rows) against a complete real `BlockChain`: sibling force-reorgs with every error kind, side chain marking round trips, a best chain invalidation whose fallback reorganization itself fails because invalidation clears the validated status and the skeleton blocks cannot pass revalidation (the chain settles on the deepest valid tip), the no-op repeat, reconsiderations that fail revalidation and stay put, and a deep invalidation taking every branch with it — comparing verdicts, tips, the status byte of every block after every operation, and the final utxo universe.  Also ported: the chain state persistence — `createChainState` (every bucket, the versioned database info rows, the genesis block index row, best state, stake database initialization, the stored genesis block and its empty filter, and the deployment version row) and the `initChainState` load path (the database, compression and block index version downgrade rejections with dcrd's messages; height-ordered block index reconstruction, including the new-consensus-rules failed-block unmarking, which stays in memory only as in dcrd: its post-load flush writes none of the unmarked rows, so a second restart before the block is revalidated reloads it as failed; the best chain and candidate restoration, the stake node load, and the best state snapshot rebuild — reproducing dcrd's own restart quirk where `NumTxns` is recomputed from the regular transaction tree only), `flushBlockIndex` over the tracked modified nodes with pruned ticket info repopulation, and write-through persistence in the connect, disconnect, and cache flush paths (best state, spend journal, ticket database rows, filters, and commitment leaves; dcrd houses the UTXO set in a separate database with its own backend while dcroxide colocates it in a dedicated bucket of the one redb database using the same pinned row formats — a fresh-sync schema decision — and the chain data is held in recent-window mirrors over those written rows and read back from the database past the window, as the `database` row describes).  dcrd's `upgradeDB` migration paths are not applicable to dcroxide's fresh-sync databases and are intentionally not ported.  Pinned by the reorganization ground truth replayed over a database-backed chain with a flush, restart, and reload at the end: the frozen final best state and utxo universe hold against the reopened chain along with the block index statuses, spend journals, filters, tip, and stake state (restarting mid-scenario is deliberately not compared against the frozen fields because utxo cache tombstones do not survive restarts, so continued reorganizations legitimately resurrect entries from the journal fraud proofs — a timing divergence dcrd itself exhibits).  Also ported: `CheckConnectBlockTemplate` (the tip-or-parent template parent rule, sanity without proof of work, the positional and contextual checks, and the full connect checks — over the as-is chain state when extending the tip and over the tip block's spend journal undo when building on its parent; templates never enter the block index so no scaffolding nodes are created), `checkTicketExhaustion`/`CheckTicketExhaustion` (the maturity-window live pool projection with the pre-stake-validation voting block adjustment), and the chain query surface (`MainChainHasBlock`, `BlockHeightByHash`, `BlockHashByHeight`, `HeaderByHash`/`Height`, `BlockByHash`/`Height`, `MedianTimeByHash`, `ChainWork`, `TipGeneration`, and `HeightRange`).  Pinned by 172 vectors against a complete real `BlockChain`: seven template verdicts (a valid template on the tip before and after the commitment root fixup, a valid template on the tip's parent exercising the journal undo path, and the invalid-parent, duplicate-transaction, height-commitment, and coinbase-subsidy rejections), eight ticket exhaustion verdicts over a header-only chain approaching stake validation height (thresholds, rescue by sufficient purchases, and the unknown block), and the query surface outputs.  Also ported: the treasury account and spend tracking from `treasury.go` — the treasury state rows (the balance as of each block plus its yet-to-mature values with dcrd's exact VLQ serialization incl. the negative-debit storage convention and sign sanity checks), the treasury-spend-to-blocks rows (corrupt rows of both kinds diverge: a negative treasury spend count, or a spend or value count too large to allocate, is reported as corruption where dcrd's `make` panics, since the port caps each reservation at what the row's bytes can hold; a value type outside 1-4 after the `0x07` type mask is also rejected as corruption, where dcrd's `deserializeTreasuryState` keeps it as a credit of that raw type; and dcrd reads both kinds per lookup, where the port loads every treasury spend row and the recent window's state rows at startup, so a corrupt one there refuses the start; state rows past the window are read per lookup as in dcrd, and `calculate_treasury_balance` reads a failed fetch as a zero balance as `calculateTreasuryBalance` does), `calculateTreasuryBalance` (the coinbase-maturity window), the per-block record writes wired into the block connect path (inside that path's single database transaction, where dcrd writes them, so a durable best state can never be missing its own treasury row -- `calculate_treasury_balance` reads a missing row as a zero balance that every descendant would then inherit, and nothing on the startup path repairs it), `checkTSpendExists` with fork awareness, `tSpendCountVotes`/`checkTSpendHasVotes` (the window walk over vote-carrying blocks with the quorum and remaining-votes short-circuit math), `sumPastTreasuryChanges`, all three expenditure policies (DCP0006 window averages with the bootstrap fallback, DCP0007 income-based, and DCP0013 balance-percentage with the spend limit floor) with the agenda-driven selection, `checkTSpendsExpenditure`, and the complete `tspendChecks` — wired into `checkConnectBlock` through its closure seam at dcrd 2.2's position (after the stake tree connect), covering the full window, duplicate-mine, vote tally, and expenditure battery (the value-in commitment check moved into `CheckTransactionInputs`' `checkTreasurySpendInputs` per dcrd 2.2).  Pinned by 264 vectors against a complete real `BlockChain`: a 240-block simnet chain with treasurybases, treasury adds, treasury-vote-carrying votes across a full voting window, and a spend mined mid-window — treasury balances at five depths, exists checks across the mine boundary, vote tallies mid-window and at the window end, maximum expenditures at two vote interval boundaries, all five `tspendChecks` verdict kinds (ok, exists, expenditure, votes, and the non-interval no-op), and the raw treasury rows byte for byte.  The DCP0006 and DCP0007 expenditure paths are exercised structurally but not pinned against dcrd on simnet since the DCP0013 agenda is force-activated there; they await the full-chain cross-validation work.  Cross-validated end to end: dcrd’s own full block test battery (`fullblocktests.Generate`) replayed through the real `ProcessBlock` with full validation on — 573 instances over a scripted regression network chain of fully signed blocks with real ticket purchases, votes, revocations, P2SH redemptions, and reorganizations: 204 accepted blocks matching dcrd’s main-chain and orphan verdicts, 350 rejections matching dcrd’s exact rule error kind across 61 distinct kinds (incl. `ErrScriptValidation` from real signature failures), the orphan-or-reject instances, 14 expected-tip checkpoints, and the non-canonical block encoding rejected at the wire layer.  The dump records its generation wall clock so the too-far-in-the-future rejection replays, and the nanosecond-precision timestamp rejection is a documented skip: wire timestamps are whole seconds by type, so the instance only exists for in-memory blocks and dcrd itself cannot hit it from serialized bytes either.  Also ported (completing the `internal/blockchain` surface): the RPC and network-sync query layer — `stateLastChanged`/`StateLastChangedHeight` (the interval-by-interval walk back from the current confirmation interval), the public `NextThresholdState`, `GetVoteCounts`/`CountVoteVersion` (invalid vote choices tallied as abstains, wrong-version votes ignored), `GetStakeVersions` (with dcrd’s count clamp and error shapes), `GetVoteInfo`, the block locator surface (`locateInventory`/`LocateBlocks`/`LocateHeaders` with the wire max-headers cap, and `BlockLocatorFromHash` falling back to the best tip), and the RPC stake difficulty estimators (`estimateNextStakeDifficultyV1` with its fake-chain overlay distributing the estimated ticket purchases across the remainder of the interval, `estimateNextStakeDifficultyV2` over the DCP0001 pool projections, and the agenda-driven `EstimateNextStakeDifficulty` selector).  Pinned by 1,464 vectors over three deterministic fake chains generated inside dcrd’s own package: a mainnet block index with a side chain replaying dcrd’s own locator test layout, a 1,200-block regnet chain that runs the DCP0001 agenda through Started, LockedIn, and Active with real votes (wrong-version and invalid-choice votes included) exercising both estimator algorithms on either side of the activation and the real state-change walks, and the simnet forced-choice fast paths — the fake chains replay hash-identical.  Also ported: the committed filter serving from `headercmt.go` — `FilterByBlockHash` (the version 2 GCS filter for a block with its header commitment inclusion proof, gated on the block being known with data available, surfacing the no-filter error kind otherwise) and `LocateCFiltersV2` (the batched cfilters response over an inclusive block range, with the unknown-block, not-an-ancestor, and over-the-maximum-batch error gates, the parent walk collecting the range hashes, and the per-filter inclusion proofs), both serving from the in-memory filter and header-commitment caches the engine keeps in step with the database; `Chain::new` now caches the genesis block's committed filter to match `createChainState` and the open path, so the genesis filter is served like any other block's.  Pinned by a real regnet chain built from dcrd's own fullblocktests generator (fifteen blocks) with dcrd's `FilterByBlockHash` and `LocateCFiltersV2` exercised directly: the block bytes are frozen so the engine here rebuilds the same chain and serves byte-identical filters, proof indices, and proof hashes across mid-chain, tip, and genesis blocks, a range and single-block batch, and the unknown-block/not-ancestor/no-filter error kinds; the server `OnGetCFilterV2`/`OnGetCFiltersV2` handlers wrap these (pushing the response on success and ignoring the error otherwise, confirmed in the dump by driving dcrd's real handlers over a piped serverPeer).  Also ported: the live-ticket queries from `stakeext.go` — `CheckLiveTicket`, `CheckLiveTickets`, and `LiveTickets` delegating to the best node's stake state, and the `BestSnapshot` accessor.  Pinned by a real regnet chain built past the stake validation height from the fullblocktests generator (one hundred fifty blocks): the full ordered live ticket set (two hundred eighty-five tickets) matches dcrd's `LiveTickets` byte for byte, the per-ticket and batched membership checks agree for live tickets, an unknown hash, and a block hash, and the best snapshot hash and height match.  Also ported: `LotteryDataForBlock` — the next tickets eligible for voting, the ticket pool size, and the lottery PRNG final state for a block's stake node, empty below stake enabled height and returning the unknown-block error otherwise.  Pinned over the same real past-stake-validation regnet chain: the tip's winning tickets, pool size, and final state match dcrd byte for byte, as do the empty-winner rows for blocks between stake enabled and stake validation heights (with their growing pool sizes), the blank data below stake enabled height and for the genesis block, and the unknown-block error.  Also ported: `TicketPoolValue` — the sum of the stake submission output amount of every live ticket, resolved through the utxo set; pinned over the same real chain against dcrd's exact total (five million seven hundred thousand atoms across two hundred eighty-five tickets).  The 2.2 campaign (upstream master `452c1a6c`) ports the consensus delta: `checkedmath.go`'s overflow-checked adders with dcrd's exact flag expressions (`addUnsigned` ok = sum ≥ a; `addSigned` ok = (sum > a) == (b > 0), over wrapping adds); the sigops rework — `countSigOps`/`countP2SHSigOps` to fallible `u32` with per-script overflow guards and master's messages, the new `CountTotalSigOps` composition, `checkNumSigOps` deleted into `checkTransactionsAndConnect`'s inline cumulative check, and the real behavior change where a vote's ticket input now contributes P2SH sigops (the old code skipped the whole vote) — with the mempool/mining/template consumers moved to `CountTotalSigOps` like upstream; `checkTransactionsAndConnect` returning the accumulated fees (`getStakeTreeFees` deleted; the stake-tree return feeds the regular tree) with `addSigned` fee accumulation and the pre-stake-validation-height expected-stakebase-output arm removed; `CheckTransactionInputs` letting treasurybases flow through the input loop (fee 0 for valid ones, range errors for malformed — the coinbase early-out now gated on !isTreasuryBase), the `sumTotalAtomIn` overflow/max closure, the vote stakebase summing its `ValueIn` commitment directly, and the new `checkTreasurySpendInputs`/`extractTreasurySpendCommitAmount` battery (input range + spend-amount commitment, absorbing the value-in check `tspendChecks` used to run); `tspendChecks` relocated after the stake-tree connect — and the port's full chain-backed battery wired into `checkConnectBlock` through a new closure seam at exactly that position, closing a gap where only the stateless subset ran in the live path — with `addSigned` total accumulation and `u32` vote tallies under `addUnsigned` overflow guards (`ErrTooManyTreasurySpendVotes`, unreachable in practice) and master's window message as a `RuleError`; `checkTreasuryBase` dropping its params argument; `checkProofOfStake`/vote/revocation context messages restated (the new revocation null-outpoint arm is unreachable defense in depth — `CheckSSRtx` forces a stake-tree input while `isNullOutpoint` requires the regular tree — pinned natively on the reachable general-rule path); and the `ErrFraudAmountIn`/`ErrInvalidTreasurySpendExpiry` kind mappings.  Pinned by re-dumping the affected corpora from a throwaway exporter inside master's `internal/blockchain`: the sigops file (one cpso vote verdict changed 0→1 per the behavior change, plus 40 new `CountTotalSigOps` rows) and the `CheckTransactionInputs` file (a degenerate treasurybase flipping ok→`ErrSpendTooHigh` and a mismatched treasury spend flipping ok→`ErrInvalidTSpendValueIn`), retiring the `checkConnectBlock` suite's stateless value-in row (the check moved out of `tspendChecks`), the unchanged suites replaying as before, plus native pins for the `checkTreasurySpendInputs` battery.  The `internal/blockchain` machinery not otherwise reproduced is documented plumbing: the database upgrade paths (fresh-sync) and the mutex/notification scaffolding.  Logging is a second, separate gap: the crate takes an injected package log sink the way dcrd's package does (`slog.Disabled` by default, installed through `UseLogger`, which `dcrd/log.go:85` calls with the CHAN logger), but only `flushBlockIndexWarnOnly`'s warning is reproduced — the rest of `internal/blockchain`'s CHAN lines (roughly 160 slog calls over the non-test sources) and all of `treasury.go`'s `trsyLog` lines are unported, as are `upgrade.go`'s (fresh-sync).  Rule-error descriptions carry dcrd's text field for field; `validate.rs` unit tests pin a sample. That includes the treasury spend `ParseSignature` wrap, which prints dcrd's `schnorr.Error` description rather than the dcrec port's kind name. dcrd prints the header and median times with `%v` in the host's local zone, because its wire decoder builds `time.Unix` values. dcroxide renders them in Go's `time.Time` format pinned to UTC (`2026-09-23 14:00:01 +0000 UTC`), so the text matches a dcrd running in UTC. The block import progress log makes the same choice. |
| `internal/blockchain/indexers` | `dcroxide-indexers` | vectors | Complete: the shared indexer machinery (the `idxtips` bucket with its 36-byte hash+height tips, the `v`-prefixed version keys and `d`-prefixed drop markers, index creation/upgrade, the interrupted-drop resume on init, the incremental flat drop in 2,000,000-entry batches, and main-chain recovery by repeated tip disconnection), the index update subscriber with prerequisite/dependent subscription chains, the `updateIndex` expected-height state machine (lower heights relayed to the dependent with the relay error deliberately discarded exactly as dcrd does, higher heights failing with `ErrMissingNotification`), and `CatchUp` from the lowest index tip; the version 2 transaction index (three buckets: 48-byte entries under `txbyhashidx` keyed by tx hash with block id/offset/length/tree index, plus the `idbyhashidx`/`hashbyididx` block-ID compaction pair, the forward-scan-plus-binary-search highest-ID recovery on init, and the `TxLoc` offsets derived from the serialized block exactly as `DeserializeTxLoc`); the version 2 exists address index (21-byte type+hash160 keys with empty values under `existsaddridx`, the four address-type foldings incl. pay-to-pubkey collapsing onto its pubkey-hash twin, multisig redeem script extraction from signature scripts, ticket commitment addresses for both P2PKH and the amount-MSB P2SH flavor, the never-remove disconnect semantics, and the memory-only unconfirmed overlay flushed into the bucket on connect); and the legacy `txbyaddridx`/`cfindexparentbucket` drop helpers.  dcrd's buffered-channel goroutine delivery and 500ms sync ticker are daemon-phase concurrency; this port delivers synchronously with identical state transitions — on the block-processing thread under the sync-manager mutex — and the sync-waiter signalling is covered natively.  Index maintenance therefore runs serially with block connection: for the default-on exists-address index, one existence probe per distinct address plus one write transaction per block. dcrd overlaps it with the next block's validation through a 128-deep channel (`bufferSize`) and a dedicated goroutine. The throughput cost is unmeasured, and moving the updates to a dedicated thread is an open decision (measure the default against `--noexistsaddrindex` first).  The interior sharing is `Arc`/`Mutex` rather than `Rc`/`RefCell` — the index handles are `Arc<Mutex<TxIndex>>`/`Arc<Mutex<ExistsAddrIndex>>`, the chain queryer trait carries a `Send + Sync` supertrait so its `Arc<dyn ChainQueryer>` handles cross threads, and the interrupt and sync waiters are `AtomicBool`s instead of `Cell`s — so the indexes are `Send` and can run under the daemon's threaded server (a compile-time assertion pins it); this is thread-safety plumbing with no behavioral change, confirmed by the unchanged vectors, and the daemon consumes it through the node crate's index wiring (see the daemon row).  Pinned by a 4-scenario scripted session generated inside dcrd's own package over its `testChain` and chaingen blocks, replayed against a real redb-backed database: creation, catch-up, connects, disconnects, a reorg with recovery on re-init, drop and re-creation, entry queries, an address-soup block covering every key type, the unconfirmed overlay, independent and dependent subscriptions incl. the lagging-dependent relay, and the error kinds — comparing the index tips and the full byte-for-byte contents of every index bucket after each operation |
| `internal/mempool` | `dcroxide-mempool` | vectors | Complete: the mempool error identities (all 22 `ErrorKind`s with dcrd’s exact names incl. the historical "ErrorAlreadyVoted" spelling, and the `RuleError` that wraps either a mempool kind or an underlying chain rule error with dcrd’s `wrapTxRuleError` kind-retention semantics) and the relay policy layer from `policy.go`: `calcMinRequiredTxRelayFee` (with the Go overflow wrap clamped to the max amount), `isDust` (the 6030-atom p2pkh boundary at the default relay fee, unspendable outputs, and wrapped arithmetic on extreme values), `checkPkScriptStandard` (version gate plus the 1-to-3-key multisig limits), `checkTransactionStandard` (serialize type, finality, size, signature script size and push-only rules with the treasury spend input skip, output standardness with the dust and null-data-count exemptions by transaction type), and `checkInputsStandard` (referenced script forms and the 15-sigop P2SH limit, with the vote and treasury-spend first-input skips; the utxo view lookup is a closure like the other engine-coupled ports).  Pinned by 69 verdicts from dcrd’s own package over its policy test tables plus boundary, overflow, and skip-semantics cases, incl. input checks against a real `UtxoViewpoint`.  Also ported: the `TxPool` itself — the main, orphan, and stage pools with their outpoint indexes, vote and treasury spend tracking, the complete acceptance gauntlet (`maybeAcceptTransaction`: duplicate and known-transaction checks, the chain sanity and input validation delegated to the ported blockchain layers, expiry, the stake-type height gates, standardness, the ticket stake difficulty gate, the mixpool pair-request check (`NonMixSpendsPairRequest`, behind an optional `MixpoolProbe` trait object standing in for dcrd's config closure, with dcrd's nil-closure semantics when absent), pool/vote/revocation double spend checks, old vote policy, the already-exists check over the fetched view, orphan detection with fraud proof refresh, sequence locks, sigop limits, both fee policy directions, script verification via the newly ported single-transaction `ValidateTransactionScripts` (sharing the block path's worker pool, engine setup and failure text, and treating only an absent view entry as missing, as dcrd's `UtxoViewpoint.PrevScript` does), and the treasury spend expiry/limit/Pi-key policy), orphan processing with cascading acceptance and double spend eviction, ticket staging with unstaging through `MaybeAcceptDependents`, batch acceptance over the transient pool (`MaybeAcceptTransactions`), the stake and expiry pruners, and the removal/query surface.  The chain callbacks live behind a `PoolChain` trait standing in for dcrd’s config closures; the fee estimator hook and notification callbacks are integration plumbing that landed with the daemon wiring (the mining view bookkeeping landed with the mempool wiring, and the pool now carries dcrd's exists-address hook: an optional `UnconfirmedAddrIndexer` trait object standing in for the concrete `ExistsAddrIndex` config field, notified with the transaction at the end of `addTransaction` exactly where dcrd calls `AddUnconfirmedTx` — dcrd has no removal hook, the overlay flushes wholesale on block connect — pinned by a native test replaying the battery's first acceptance against a recording index), and dcrd’s time-driven orphan expiration and randomized orphan eviction are ported but deliberately untriggered by the vectors since dcrd itself documents the eviction order as arbitrary.  Pinned by a 90-row scripted session against dcrd’s own pool test harness (`newPoolHarness` with real signed transactions over a mirrored fake chain): chained acceptance, orphan cascades and policy, duplicates, double spends, already-exists, fee policy both directions, block- and time-based sequence locks, expiry with pruning, ticket staging and unstaging on parent confirmation, stake difficulty gates with pruning, batch acceptance, and double spend removal — comparing every verdict, accepted list, and the full pool/orphan/stage state after every operation.  Notable dump landmine: the coinbase height lives in the signature script, which is witness data excluded from the transaction hash, so distinct funding coinbases must vary their prefixes or they alias the same outpoints.  Also pinned: the stake acceptance scenarios over a second 137-row scripted session from dcrd’s own harness — the vote orphan flow (vote rejected as an orphan, the ticket arriving as an orphan itself, the funding transaction cascading the ticket into the stage pool, and the mined ticket unlocking the vote), duplicate votes on the same block, the five-vote double spend limit across candidate blocks with recovery after removal, old vote policy, the vote and revocation stake validation height gates, revocation acceptance alongside votes with the one-revocation-per-ticket rule, disapproval tallies over no-votes, the votes-map retention quirk after pruning (dcrd deliberately keeps vote metadata for blocks after the votes leave the pool), and the treasury battery with a replaced Pi key set: the seven-concurrent-tspend limit with recovery, mined-on-ancestor rejection, the expiry policy in all three directions, the chain-level `ErrInvalidPiSignature`/`ErrUnknownPiKey` rejections, acceptance up to the final voting window block, treasury adds under the fee policy, and standalone treasurybase rejection.  The pool and the mining transaction graph are `Send` for the threaded daemon (shared descriptors on `Arc` instead of `Rc`, the chain parameters owned instead of borrowed, and an owned subsidy-parameter source), pinned by a compile-time assertion; the frozen vectors confirm behavior unchanged.  The pool now runs in the daemon: a chain adapter binds every seam to the live chain (the utxo view for a transaction's inputs and own outputs with the disapproved-tip-tree disconnect (memoized per tip like dcrd's `disapprovedView`; the port keeps one memo per chain adapter rather than one on the chain, which serves the identical view) and the view's missing outpoints resolved in one batched cache/backend read (dcrd `fetchUtxosMain` over `FetchEntries`), the sequence-lock calculation over the tip branch, the six agenda queries at the tip, the tspend-mined-on-ancestor walk, and dcrd's standard script verify flags with the LN-features and treasury additions), the policy carries dcrd's exact values (orphan size = the standard tx cap, sigops = a fifth of the block cap, the per-network vote age switch), the mixpool probe is installed by the shared mixing pool's own constructor so no daemon path can build a pool that skips the pair-request step, the netsync manager drives the shared pool instead of the null stub, and the RPC server serves it (getrawmempool, mempool counts, tspend hashes, verbose descriptors with derived dependencies (one entry per redeeming input, so a parent spent twice is listed twice, as dcrd's `VerboseTxDescs` appends them; the RPC descriptors carry the serialized size rather than a copy of the transaction), the gettxout mempool probe, and sendrawtransaction submitting through the pool with dcrd's duplicate/rule error mapping over the recently-confirmed filter).  The mempool now serves the network too: a mempool request answers the pool's inventory through the ported flood guard (queued at once in inv messages of at most dcrd's `maxInvTrickleSize`, 1000 vectors: the batches dcrd's trickle timer would send on its next tick), a transaction getdata serves from the pool only — confirmed transactions are deliberately not servable, matching dcrd — and answers notfound otherwise, and a transaction accepted off the wire feeds the websocket txaccepted/relevant-tx notifications (dcrd `AnnounceNewTransactions`' notification half).  Accepted transactions now relay to peers too: every registered peer carries its relay state (the handshake facts including the remote's disable-relay bit read off the version message, the last announced block, and a known-inventory set that dedups announcements and never echoes inventory the peer itself announced), the fan-out drives the ported per-peer relay decision (where dcrd trickles non-immediate inventory over a random window, the plain per-peer queue sends each announcement as its own message), announced transactions join the recently-advertised cache (4500 entries, 45 seconds) that getdata consults before the pool, and RPC-submitted transactions announce through the connection manager's relay seam.  The pool also follows the chain now: a connected block's transactions leave the pool (without evicting their now-valid redeemers), staged tickets unstage, conflicting pool transactions and matching orphans evict, and newly acceptable orphans run the full announce cascade; a block that disapproves its parent returns the parent's regular transactions to contention, and a disconnected block's transactions re-admit — all deferred through the chain event handler and drained after each processing call, since the pool reaches back into the chain the callback holds.  The rebroadcast machinery (the timer thread with its stake-aware prunes), block announcements over the relay, and the fee estimator hooks all run in the daemon now, each detailed in the daemon row |

Also ported: the background template generator's regeneration state machine (`bgblktmplgenerator`) — the regen handler state with its timers modeled as armed flags whose real countdowns the daemon drives, the block connect/disconnect/accept, vote, template-update, and force-regen event handlers with the reorg bracket and the unsynced gating, the minimum-votes lock-in ladder with the max-votes propagation timeout (a vote below the maximum with the timeout armed is deliberately a no-op), side chain vote tracking with vote-driven and timeout-driven forced reorganizations (a failed reorg candidate falls through to monitoring, and with no template yet one is generated on the parent), the notified-parents LRU of size 3 with the new-votes→new-parent first-notification upgrade, and the stale-template wait counting. Notable dump landmines: `dcrutil.NewBlock` caches the block hash eagerly, so sibling variants must bake distinct nonces into the header before construction (a nonce set afterward silently leaves the hash — and the scenario — degenerate); the reorg-started path clears the template synchronously through the unbuffered regen event queue, so an in-package driver must drain concurrently or deadlock; and the dump blocks' zero `time.Time` serializes as `uint32(-62135596800)` in the header codec, which participates in their hashes.  The concurrency shell — the regen queue and subscription fan-out goroutines, the real timers, and the blocking template retrieval waits — is not in this crate; it lives in the daemon's `bgtemplate.rs`, which drives this synchronous state machine from a dedicated thread (see the daemon row) |
| `internal/ratelimit` | `dcroxide-ratelimit` | vectors | Complete (new in dcrd 2.2; ported from master `452c1a6c`, the campaign parity target): the token bucket rate limiter behind the rewritten connection manager's inbound anti-flood machinery — `New`/`Allow`/`Tokens`/`Burst`/`UntilNextAllowed` with dcrd's exact `f64` bucket arithmetic in dcrd's operation order, incl. `Duration.Seconds()`'s whole-second/remainder-nanosecond split, the burst cap, the clock-going-backwards refill freeze, and the `UntilNextAllowed` overflow guard returning `Forever`.  Divergences per the port's conventions: dcrd's struct-embedded `nowFn` becomes an explicit monotonic `now_nanos` argument on every time-dependent call (the daemon feeds the inbound group and drop-log limiters `dcroxide_connmgr::monotonic_nanos`, a process-`Instant` reading, because Go's `Time.Sub` of two `time.Now` values is monotonic; `runtime.rs`'s `admit_inbound_now` reads the clocks for `ConnManager::admit_inbound`, and `log_inbound_drop` reads its own, as dcrd's `LogDrops` does; the wall clock is kept only where dcrd reads it: the limiter cache's TTL, which `container/lru` keeps in `UnixNano`, and the flood window's `Unix()` seconds (`InboundRateLimiter::allow(addr, now_unix, now_nanos, mono_nanos)`); the persistent-peer backoff's `time.Since(lastAttempt)` is stamped and compared on the same monotonic clock; tests at each of these call sites fail when that site reads the wall clock), the embedded mutex is left to consumers (the core is single-threaded like the rest of the port), dcrd's zero-`time.Time` initial `updated` is an `i64::MIN` sentinel that yields the saturated `MaxInt64` refill interval for every clock value — incl. after an `allow` at the sentinel itself, which dcrd likewise cannot distinguish from the initial state — and the overflow guard's `uint64(float64)` conversion (platform-defined in Go: NaN → guard fires on amd64 but not arm64) pins the amd64 oracle platform, so NaN and `+Inf` candidates return `Forever`.  Pinned by a 189-line scripted-clock dump from dcrd's own package (throwaway exporter in the clone) across twenty-one scenarios — the connmgr group and drop-log constants, fractional refill drift (incl. a case where accumulated rounding denies an event dcrd's documented average rate would allow), zero/negative-zero/negative/subnormal/NaN/infinite/huge rates (a `1e300` rate overflows the refill delta to `+Inf` before the burst cap), zero and `u32::MAX` bursts, clock reversal on both the failing and succeeding `Allow` paths, first-use far from the zero value, and the rates at the `int64` nanosecond horizon (`1e-9` tokens/s truncates to `999999999999999872` ns; `1e-10` and below return `Forever`) — every post-op token count and update stamp compared bit for bit, plus a native test pinning the sentinel-stamp corner against bits probed from dcrd's package directly (unreachable by the exporter's base-plus-offset clock) |
| `mixing`, `mixing/mixpool`, `mixing/utxoproof` | `dcroxide-mixing` | vectors | The mixing library primitives — the `Message` interface as a `MixMessage` trait over the eight wire messages, message signing and verification (the signed-data BLAKE-256 digest fed into the tagged, comma-joined `schnorrHash` with the pair request's zero session and run, then EC-Schnorr-DCRv0), session ID derivation and pair request session sorting (the lexicographic pre-sort, the epoch-tagged digest, and the sid-XOR re-sort into unmixed positions) with key exchange session validation and its two error identities, the DC-net math over F = 2**127-1 (`SRMixPads`/`SRMix`/`AddVectors`/`Coefficients`/`IsRoot`, `DCMixPads`/`DCMix`, the message vectors with XOR combining) carried as canonical residues in a Mersenne-folding u128 field — dcrd computes over unreduced `math/big` integers with a final `Mod(F)`, and every dcrd-observable output is that canonical residue — the per-run ChaCha20 PRNG, the UTXO ownership proofs (`utxoproof`), `MaxExpiry`, and the mixing limits/flags/script class constants.  Pinned by a scripted session generated inside dcrd's own mixing package: the identity hash and signed-data digest of all eight messages, deterministic RFC6979 signatures byte for byte with verification and a tampering rejection, session sorting and validation across its error cases, a full honest 4-peer exponential and XOR DC-net run (pads, mixes, power sums, coefficients, and root checks with recovery of every peer message), field membership, PRNG keystreams, ownership proofs both directions, and expiries across three networks.  Also ported: `mixpool` — the in-memory pool of mixing messages with the full acceptance gauntlet (signature verification, the pair request battery: change dust/version/script checks, missing UTXOs, message counts, mix dust, total amounts, the expiry window, script class, the fee band, and the UTXO checks over an optional fetcher trait incl. duplicate outpoints, unsupported P2SH, spent/unconfirmed/wrong-version outputs, outputs an unmined transaction already spends (dcrd's `mixpoolChain.FetchUtxoEntry` asks `mempool.IsSpent` before the chain and returns a nil entry, which `mixpool.go:1448` folds into the same rejection as a spent one — the port takes that answer as a predicate computed by the caller instead: dcrd reaches the fetcher from `checkAcceptPR`, which `AcceptMessage` runs before it takes the mixpool's mutex (`mixpool.go:1198-1210`), but the port's acceptance runs wholly under the mixpool guard, so asking from inside would take the transaction pool's lock while holding the mixpool's, closing a cycle against the acceptance gauntlet's mixpool probe, which takes them the other way round; the consequence is that the port asks the question before the pool's own rerun, already-accepted and pair-request checks (and before it consults the signature verdict it was handed), where dcrd asks after all of them, bounded by the wire's 512-outpoint cap on a pair request and pinned by a lock-order test that deadlocks on the other arrangement.  The message itself is handled in dcrd's lock scope: the input loop hashes each received mix message once, as it is read and before any lock, and the handler verifies its signature once, with no peer lock held and before the sync-manager and mixpool locks (`HashedMessage`), as dcrd's `inHandler` and `AcceptMessage` do; the pair-request checks — the chain UTXO fetches and one ownership-proof verification per claimed UTXO — still run under the mixpool guard where dcrd runs them unlocked, so validating a large pair request briefly holds up the mixpool's other users (getdata serving, `MisbehavingBlock`, the mempool's `NonMixSpendsPR` probe)), the opcode-dispatched ownership proof over regular and stake script kinds, and the summed value match; key exchange validation with session checks, position bounds, the early-epoch gate, own-PR-missing orphaning with `MissingOwnPRError`, identity and pairing compatibility; the per-type message limits with dcrd's exact messages incl. the CT limit message that prints the ciphertext count where the maximum belongs; conflict detection; and orphan handling with reconsideration cascades and source-based eviction), sessions with expiry (QK-0002, now retired: `release-v2.1.5` created every session with the maximum expiry because the slice it folded over was never appended to, so sessions only died through pair request expiry; dcrd fixed it in `d11ae7af`, which is in the current target, and `accept_ke` now folds `pr.Expires()` into the running minimum over the KE's known referenced pair requests exactly as master does, with the mixpool vectors regenerated against master so the session rows carry real expiries), the removal surface (rejected messages, sessions, confirmed sessions and mixes, spent and unresponsive pair requests) backed by the recently-removed LRU cache from dcroxide-containers, the pairing queries (`MixPRs`/`CompatiblePRs` with the expiry-ordered double-spend filter/`ReceiveKEsByPairing`), the receive collection semantics (QK-0003: dcrd's capacity-misuse error path leaks its read lock; the synchronous port returns the error), and the misbehavior observer (`CheckPrevEpoch` with the timeout blame analysis, strike sets with common-ownership merging, `MisbehavingTx`/`MisbehavingBlock`/`ExcludePRs`, and strike removal through completed mixes).  dcrd's mutexes, blocking `Receive` broadcast, background expiry task, and observer epoch ticker are daemon-phase concurrency: the port is synchronous with injectable wall and monotonic clocks (orphan age and the recently-removed cache's TTL use the monotonic one, as Go's `time.Since` and `lru` comparisons over `time.Now()` readings do; key exchange epochs use the wall clock, as dcrd's `time.Unix` comparisons do, with Go's internal-seconds arithmetic reproduced for out-of-range epochs), `receive` collects what is currently accepted (dcrd's pre-cancelled-context path, which its own observer uses), and the scheduled expiry latch is exposed for the daemon to drive.
