| `addrmgr` / `connmgr` / `peer` | `dcroxide-addrmgr` / `-connmgr` / `-peer` | vectors | Complete: `addrmgr` — network address types with dcrd's key and group key formatting (Go's `net.IP.String` semantics incl. the dotted rendering of IPv4-mapped forms and RFC5952 IPv6, `JoinHostPort` bracketing, the /16 IPv4 and /32 (/36 he.net) IPv6 groups with the tunnel extractions for 6to4/Teredo/RFC6052/RFC6145), the full RFC-range classification and routability rules, the reachability lattice and external-address candidacy, known-address viability (`chance` bit for bit and `isBad` across all five criteria), the manager itself (new/tried buckets over the double-BLAKE-256 bucket derivations, add with the 2N re-add likelihood, bad-then-oldest new-bucket expiry, attempt/connected/good transitions incl. the tried-bucket eviction that swaps the oldest tried address back into a new bucket, the address cache with its 23%-capped random subset, local address bookkeeping with priority scoring and best-local selection), and the `peers.json` serialization, written via serde with dcrd's exact field names — incl. QK-0004: dcrd never restores the serialized address timestamps on load, so loaded addresses are re-stamped with the load time (Go's zero time for the attempt/success fields round-trips exactly through its `Unix()` encoding, modeled as an optional).  The file is read with `json.NewDecoder(r).Decode` semantics. The first JSON value is framed as `Decoder.readValue` frames it. A file with nothing but whitespace is `EOF`. A syntax error is the scanner's first one inside the value, reported at the byte that breaks it. A value the file cuts short is `unexpected EOF`. A complete value is decoded without what follows it, and a number or literal ends at the first byte that cannot continue it. The value is then decoded through `dcroxide_dcrjson::gojson` into a Go type mirroring `serializedAddrManager`. Field names match case-insensitively and the last duplicate key wins. `null` and missing fields keep their zero values. `Version` and `Attempts` are 64-bit Go `int`s, so `KnownAddress.attempts` is `i64` and wraps on increment. `Key [32]byte` and the bucket arrays zero-fill or truncate. All error texts are Go 1.26's, the toolchain dcrd's release image uses. `load_peers` returns what dcrd's `loadPeers` logs: `Loaded %d addresses from file '%s'` from `numAddresses()`, or `Failed to parse file %s: %v` and `Failed to remove corrupt peers file %s: %v`. Open, read and remove failures carry Go's `*os.PathError` text (`open <path>: permission denied`, `read <path>: is a directory`, `remove <path>: directory not empty`), with the errno spelled as Go's `syscall.Errno` spells it on Unix. The removal ports `os.Remove`'s unlink-then-rmdir, so an empty `peers.json` directory is removed. A failed load keeps the counts it reached (QK-0023). Divergences: (1) a `null` entry in `Addresses` is a nil-pointer dereference that panics dcrd at every startup, since the file is never removed; the port rejects the file, removes it and starts empty. (2) With a duplicate `Addresses` key, Go's decoder reuses the structs the first occurrence allocated, so fields the second occurrence omits keep the first's values; gojson decodes each occurrence fresh. (3) A `LastAttempt`/`LastSuccess` outside the `i64`-nanosecond range (before 1678 or after 2262) saturates on load. `chance` and `isBad` stay exact, because `chance` compares with `saturating_sub` as `time.Since` saturates, but a later save writes the saturated seconds and not the file's value. (4) Off Unix, the file-error texts keep the system's message as Rust reports it, without the `(os error N)` suffix, and the removal does not reproduce `os.Remove`'s Windows attribute handling. (5) A read error partway through the file is reported as the read error even when the bytes read before it already hold a complete value or a syntax error, which Go's decoder would report first; this case is unreachable on a regular file.  dcrd's mutexes and the peers-file ticker goroutine are daemon-phase concurrency: the port is synchronous, and both the clock and the random source are injectable traits (dcrd's `crypto/rand` calls are not reproducible even in-package, so the dump pins every deterministic surface under a pinned bucket key and a lowered tried bucket size, and the randomized selection paths are covered natively with a scripted RNG).  The interior sharing is `Arc`/`Mutex` rather than `Rc`/`RefCell` — the clock is `Arc<dyn Fn + Send + Sync>`, the random source `Arc<Mutex<dyn AddrRng + Send>>`, and each shared known address `Arc<Mutex<KnownAddress>>` — so the manager is `Send` and can live in the daemon's shared server state across the peer threads (a compile-time assertion pins it); this is thread-safety plumbing with no behavioral change, confirmed by the unchanged vectors.  Pinned by a scripted session generated inside dcrd's own package: 27-address key/group/routability grids, the reachability and candidacy lattice, bucket derivations under the pinned key, manager state transitions through adds, attempts, connections, and good promotions incl. the eviction path with full per-address state comparison after every operation, viability values bit for bit through crafted serialized state, cross-loading dcrd's own `peers.json` output plus a save/load round trip, and the deserialization error cases.  The 2.2 campaign (upstream master 452c1a6c) ports the addrmgr v2 delta: Tor v3 onion addresses as a first-class type (`TorV3Address = 4` — value 3 stays retired for TorV2 — with the SHA3-256 `.onion checksum` derivation, 35-byte pubkey/checksum/version validation, the lowercase-base32 onion rendering in `Key`/`ipString`, the `torv3:<pubkey[0]&0xf>` group key checked before the IP-based groups, and `EncodeHost`'s onion branch that uppercases the 56 data characters for Go's standard base32 decode before falling back to IP parsing), per-bucket address-type statistics (`bucketStats` counters over every new/tried bucket, incremented and decremented at dcrd's exact add/expire/promote/evict/deserialize sites with Go's wrapping `uint16` arithmetic), and the filtered `GetAddress(filterFn)` selection (the all-false filter and no-matching-bucket early returns, bucket-index collection from the stats, the 50% tried/new split over matching buckets only, the nth-matching-entry walk, and the unchanged 1.2-factor chance-accept loop), plus the reachability-lattice delta (the new `Private` reach state for TorV3-to-TorV3 and the three TorV3 arms in `getRemoteReachabilityFromLocal`, checked before the RFC4380 case exactly as dcrd orders them; dcrd master's `NetAddress.Network()` net.Addr shim has no port equivalent yet — nothing consumes it before the piece-177/178 daemon surfaces); the daemon's `newAddressFunc` passes dcrd server.go's filter (IPv4/IPv6 always, TorV3 only when onion reachability is configured: `!noonion` and a proxy or onion proxy set).  dcrd 2.2 also relocated `seed.go` and `tor.go`→`tordns.go` from connmgr into addrmgr verbatim; the port moves them into `dcroxide-addrmgr` accordingly (the `ErrTor*` kinds join addrmgr's error kinds, and the reflected JSON node type in seeder decode errors becomes `addrmgr.node`), with the frozen tor/seed vector rows relocated alongside.  Pinned by a dump generated inside dcrd's addrmgr package at master 452c1a6c: onion key renderings over deterministic pubkeys, the `EncodeHost` acceptance matrix (valid/uppercase/corrupt-checksum/wrong-length/bad-alphabet/case-sensitive-suffix onion hosts plus the IP forms), the per-bucket statistics after scripted adds and one-per-type tried promotions under a pinned bucket key, the full 36-pair TorV3 reachability matrix, and the filtered selection — the dump caught three real port bugs before they shipped (a rotated chars-per-chunk table in the base32 encoder, TorV3 mis-assigned the retired value 3, and a missing `torv3:` group-key arm).  `connmgr` is also complete in `dcroxide-connmgr` — the dynamic ban score (persistent + exponentially decaying transient components with the 60s half-life, 1800s lifetime, and `uint32` truncation/wrapping semantics) over a bit-exact port of Go's portable `math.Exp`/`math.Ldexp` — incl. QK-0006: Go dispatches `Exp` to platform assembly that differs from the portable code by one ulp on 276 of the 1801 possible decay ages, so dcrd's ban scores are already platform-dependent and the port follows the portable source; the connection manager itself, as the synchronous decision core of dcrd 2.2's `internal/connmgr` described under the 2.2 connmgr rewrite below (dcrd's `Connect`/`AddPersistent`/`Disconnect`/`Remove` gates, the exponential `backoffWithJitter` retries, and the 25-failure pause in the daemon's outbound driver), with the daemon executing the dials, closes and timers the core decides. The 2.2 campaign (upstream master 452c1a6c) ports the peer rework: the four-step handshake with the verack exchange inside it (inbound reads the version, writes its own, writes verack, then reads the remote verack; outbound writes first and reads the remote verack before writing its own), strict at the negotiated addrv2 version — any non-verack second message disconnects with `ErrNotVerAckMessage` — and tolerant below it (up to three non-verack messages are queued and replayed through the input handler after the handshake, dcrd's `delayedHandshakeMsgs`); the typed handshake error kinds from dcrd 2.2's new `peer/error.go`; the version callback fired from inside the version read after the peer state updates and before the too-old rejection, so the daemon's `OnVersion` rejections abort the handshake before any verack; the peer's network address as a `wire.NetAddressV2` (the local version message converts it to the legacy `addr_you` with Go's `To16` semantics — the mapped form for 4 encoded bytes, unchanged for 16, and all-zero bytes for any other length, notably a 32-byte Tor v3 key — and the proxy hiding compares the converted form); `MaxProtocolVersion` at the addrv2 version 12; the remote services zeroed until the version message arrives (pre-2.2 seeded the local services); and `PushAddrV2Msg` (copy, shuffle, and truncate past 1000, returning the list actually sent). `Config.MaxPeers` is unused by dcrd's manager logic and has no equivalent; the sync-status logging (dcrd's `progressLogger` calls and the manager's info/warn level lines) is emitted as `Log`/`LogBlockProgress`/`LogHeaderProgress`/`ResetProgressLogTime` actions carrying dcrd's exact strings and per-call facts — the daemon renders them under the SYNC tag with `internal/progresslog` ported as the node's `ProgressLogger` (ten-second throttle, accumulation, and byte-exact templates; pinned by native throttle/format tests, a Go-generated `time.Duration.String` interval table for the `New block` line, and log-line pins across the connect, headers-synced-transition, and block paths, plus a live testnet run); the manager's debug/trace-level lines are not ported (below the default logging level); the error-level lines for failures that are not rule errors (`Failed to process transaction`, `Failed to process block header ... -- disconnecting`, `Failed to process block`) and the corruption-only `Critical failure` line (the header path's `Criticial failure` spelling included) are emitted from a rule/non-rule/corruption classification the daemon's adapters reconstruct, because the chain hands storage faults over as `RuleError`s: the store's fatal latch and the error kind mark failures that are not rule errors, and corruption is `ErrUtxoBackendCorruption` (except the missing-input `AssertError` stand-in) or a database `ErrCorruption` carried by `persist_rule_error` (read back by `process::is_persisted_db_corruption`); a corrupt or unreadable UTXO row is the exception, since `Chain::backend_fetch_entry` and its batch counterpart still abort the process where dcrd's `dbFetchUtxoEntry` returns `ErrUtxoBackendCorruption` (or the backend's read error) and its sync manager logs `Failed to process block` and `Critical failure`, so the line is reachable here only for spend-journal corruption and for database corruption surfaced by the chain's persistence transactions; the defensive `Unable to retrieve known good header` warning is unreachable here (the header seam returns an option rather than dcrd's error value), and because the daemon executes actions after releasing the manager lock, log lines from different peers may interleave across the initial-chain-sync transition in an order dcrd's transition mutex forbids (output order only; the progress totals are unaffected).  dcrd keys its peer set and request maps by pointer with random map iteration, so sync-peer ties, replacement-announcer choice, and re-request entry order are arbitrary there — iteration here is deterministic (documented at each site) and the dump avoids the ambiguous cases.  `internal/blockchain` gained `PutNextNeededBlocks` (the sliding-window walk over the index). checked against 3M Go-formatted float64/float32 values) with the e-form cutoffs (`abs < 1e-6 \|\| >= 1e21` for JSON with the `e-0X` cleanup; decimal exponent `< -4 \|\| >= 6` for `%v` in usage/help text), bytewise-sorted map keys, declaration-order struct fields with `omitempty`, `[]byte` as base64, and a Go-faithful JSON validator/decoder producing `json.UnmarshalTypeError` values (`number 3.14`, leaf types from nested failures) and scanner syntax messages (`unexpected end of JSON input`, `invalid character 'x' ...` with the byte quoted as `quoteChar` quotes it, and the `invalid character ' ' ...` that input cut off inside a number, literal, string escape or `\u` escape reports through `scanner.eof`'s synthetic space) byte for byte, as Go 1.26's v1 `encoding/json` produces them (the Go release dcrd's release image builds with, 1.26.2, rather than the module's `go` line: Go 1.27's default `GOEXPERIMENT=jsonv2` words the syntax errors differently -- a control character in a string is `in string` rather than `in string literal`, a token cut short is `unexpected end of JSON input` rather than the synthetic-space `invalid character ' '`, and a high byte is quoted `'\xff'` -- while the `UnmarshalTypeError` `Type`/`Value` pair the parameter type errors are built from is unchanged; the pin-bump procedure above says what a move to 1.27 entails); struct keys fold as `foldName` does (U+017F and U+212A match `s` and `k`).  On that base: the registry (`Register` with the full validation gauntlet and its error strings, usage-flag battery incl. the `String()` rendering with unknown-bit hex tails, `(dynamic type, string)` method keying so a plain-string lookup of a named-type method correctly misses, `RegisteredMethods` filtering), `MarshalCmd` (positional params cut at the first nil optional), `ParseParams` (defaults populated only for absent trailing params — an explicit `null` leaves the field nil with no default, pinned), `NewCmd` with the whole `assignField` conversion matrix (numeric widths with Go's overflow checks, `ParseInt` base-0 semantics incl. hex, `ParseBool`/`ParseFloat` acceptance, string-to-compound via JSON), `MethodUsageText` (incl. the English plural-to-singular squashing that turns `names` into `"nam"`), `GenerateHelp` over result-type descriptors with a port of Go's `text/tabwriter` column-block algorithm (per-block widths, trailing cells unpadded — the help output incl. the misaligned map-entry lines matches byte for byte), the JSON-RPC `Request`/`Response`/`RPCError` marshalling with all the error-code constants, and the concatenated-hashes helpers. The connection shell (the in/out/queue handler goroutines and the notification fan-out) has no synchronous counterpart.  Pinned by a 19-scenario dump generated inside dcrd's own package whose steps share one client so the filter state carries across calls, discovering transactions from real mainnet block 432,100.  The websocket notification builders follow: `subscribedClients` (per-client relevance over the filters, including the ticket commitment address path where OP_RETURN commitments resolve to their embedded addresses without watching the unspendable output) and the payload construction for every notification — blockconnected with per-client relevant transactions, blockdisconnected, work (the three template update reason strings plus the template pool insert and new-parent pruning), tspend, reorganization, winningtickets (the ticket map keyed by decimal indexes), newtickets, txaccepted and the shared txacceptedverbose payload over the full raw transaction result, relevanttxaccepted with output watching, and mixmessage — each marshalled exactly like dcrd's `MarshalCmd("1.0", nil, ntfn)` requests and returned per client for the daemon's fan-out.  Pinned by a 14-event dump driving dcrd's own builders over clients with buffered channels and comparing every drained notification byte for byte.  The live HTTP/websocket connection shells are served by the daemon's RPC listener (TLS over the generated certificate pair, the RFC 6455 upgrade at `/ws`, the request-read watchdog, and the shutdown drain — detailed in the daemon row).  The 2.2 campaign (upstream master `452c1a6c`) ports the rpcserver delta: the addnode/node handlers map connection failures through dcrd's context-aware branches.  A one-try dial whose TCP connect (direct, or to the SOCKS proxy) runs out the dial timeout answers `rpcCancelError("<subcmd>: timeout connecting to <addr>")`.  An attempt canceled while dialing answers `rpcCancelError("<subcmd>: connection attempt to <addr> canceled")`.  The driver reports these two as Go's context texts, `CONNECT_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED`/`CONNECT_CANCELED` in `dcroxide_rpc::server`.  Every other failure takes the internal-error branch, including a SOCKS handshake that outlasts the deadline and an OS-level ETIMEDOUT (neither is `context.DeadlineExceeded` in dcrd).  Its wire message is the dialer's own error text, exactly as dcrd's `Connect` returns it (dcrd's "failed operation on" prefix is log-only per `rpcInternalErr`): `tor has been disabled` for an onion target under `--noonion`, go-socks's reply-status texts through a proxy, and Go's `dial tcp <addr>: connect: <errno text>` for a failed connect, direct or to the proxy, with the first address's error when a proxy name resolves to several addresses.  On Windows a failed connect keeps std's `<message> (os error N)`, where Go writes `dial tcp <addr>: connectex: <message>`.  dcrd's first arm, the request's own context ending (`rpcConnectionClosedError`), is unrepresentable: the port's handlers run without a request context.  node's disconnect/remove keep their per-arm invalid-parameter fallthroughs and the lowercased "invalid address or node ID"; `gettreasuryspendvotes` carries dcrd 2.2's u32 vote tallies through the `TSpendCountVotes` seam (cast to i64 in the result) and the live `NodeRpcChain` bridge now implements the seam over the chain's window walk with the `ErrUnknownBlock` classification — previously an unimplemented panic on the live path; the debuglevel synopsis drops the retired ADXR subsystem; the remaining master diff is mechanical (`interface{}`→`any`, helper renames to the Go 1.21 builtins, the `blake256.NewHasher256` constructor) with no port impact.  The affected frozen rows were updated in place (the addnode dial-failure row to the internal-error shape, the lowercased node row, and the debuglevel help row) The websocket transport runs dcrd's `inHandler` ladder rather than the HTTP `processRequest` one, which the port had modelled for both: `inHandler` answers a malformed empty method before authentication and leaves the socket open, gates id-less notifications ahead of the authenticate arm so an id-less `authenticate` never authenticates anyone, runs `parseCmd` and answers its error before the `rpcLimited` gate, and processes `[`-prefixed messages entry by entry into an array reply — including its batch-only arms, where a missing `params` array is malformed and the limited gate passes the request's version through rather than the empty one the single arm sends. Reported as RVW-001, RVW-002 and RVW-003 by an external review of `382864f5`; pinned by the six websocket ladder tests in `wsrpc.rs`, each of which fails against the HTTP ordering. |
| `rpcclient` | `dcroxide-rpcclient` | — | Not started, and the only ledger row that is outstanding work rather than a settled outcome: the typed Rust client is a deliverable in its own right (brief, Phase 13), and nothing in the daemon depends on it |
| `container/apbf`, `container/lru` | `dcroxide-containers` | vectors | Complete: the age-partitioned bloom filter (the false positive rate recursion incl. dcrd's uint8 `2*k` in the fill ratio, which wraps for k >= 128 so that k == 128 yields a NaN rate, near-optimal K/L derivation incl. the L=100 brute-force cap, capacity/size/bits-per-slice derivations, enhanced double hashing over 128-bit SipHash-2-4 with dcrd's exact wrapping arithmetic incl. the deliberate slice-0 underflow in the closed formula, Lemire fast reduction, the ring-buffer generation aging with non-byte-aligned slice clearing, the consecutive-match query with its backward-jump optimization, and the deterministic reset key derivation) and the generic LRU map and set (recency ordering with in-place updates, eviction at the limit with element reuse — an index arena stands in for Go's pointer-linked list — default and per-item TTLs with the zero-TTL-means-100-years encoding, strictly-greater expiry, the invisibility of expired-but-unscanned items, the 30-second lazy expiration scans, immediate eviction, the hit ratio with its no-lookup 100% convention, and clearing).  dcrd's mutexes are daemon-phase concurrency; the ports are single-threaded with identical semantics, and the APBF hash keys and LRU clock are injectable for the tests (the LRU clock is `Arc<dyn Fn + Send + Sync>` so the caches inside a peer can move between the daemon's per-peer threads).  Pinned by scripted sessions generated inside dcrd's own packages: the siphash word mapping, the FP-rate grid bit for bit (plus dcrd-generated rows for k = 127..255 and wide-k `NewFilter`/`NewFilterKL` construction in `tests/review_apbf_wide_k.rs`, since the frozen grid stops at k = 12), construction metadata across dcrd's own parameter tables, add/probe/age/reset sessions with injected keys comparing the packed ring buffer byte for byte and the membership verdicts — false positives included — bit for bit, and an LRU battery over a mock clock comparing length, LRU-to-MRU order, eviction counts, and the hit ratio after every operation |
| `certgen` | `dcroxide-certgen` | vectors | Complete: self-signed TLS certificate pair generation for the RPC server — `NewTLSCertPair` over the two curves dcrd's `--tlscurve` accepts (P-256, the default, and P-521) and `NewEd25519TLSCertPair` — with the wall clock, key/serial randomness, hostname, and interface addresses injectable.  On Linux the daemon injects the host name and interface addresses from the kernel, where dcrd uses `os.Hostname` and `net.InterfaceAddrs`.  The host name comes from `/proc/sys/kernel/hostname` (uname's nodename).  The interface addresses are the `/32 host LOCAL` leaves of `/proc/net/fib_trie` and the rows of `/proc/net/if_inet6`.  For other platforms, see Known remaining gaps.  The certificate bytes come from a minimal DER writer reproducing Go `x509.CreateCertificate` exactly for this template shape: UTCTime validity, PrintableString/UTF8String subject selection as Go's marshaller makes it (never PrintableString for `*` or `&`), the CommonName left out when the hostname is empty, the critical KeyUsage and BasicConstraints extensions, the auto-computed SubjectKeyIdentifier Go adds for CA certificates (the leftmost 160 bits of SHA-256 over the public key: Go 1.25's default under GODEBUG `x509sha256skid`, which dcrd's root module gets from its `go 1.25.0` line; the SHA-1 form the vectors carried until 2026-09-23 was the certgen module's own `go 1.18` default, not the dcrd binary's), the SAN with hostname/localhost/interface/extra-host gathering incl. Go's `IP.Equal` deduplication and 4-byte IPv4 SAN encoding, the ASN.1-end-of-time clamping, Go `idna.ToASCII`'s bare Punycode conversion of non-ASCII names (case kept, no UTS-46 mapping or bidi/joiner checks; `punycode.rs`; QK-0007: the Ed25519 variant lacks that conversion and fails on non-ASCII hostnames, ported bug for bug), and Go `SplitHostPort`'s stray-bracket errors for extra hosts.  Keys marshal as RFC 5915 SEC1 (ECDSA) and PKCS#8 (Ed25519) with Go's PEM formatting.  Pinned by a scripted session mirroring dcrd's template-building lines with fixed inputs: Ed25519 certificates byte for byte incl. the deterministic signature across host/serial/clamping variants, ECDSA to-be-signed bytes and key encodings byte for byte for both curves (Go randomizes ECDSA signatures; the port's self-signature is verified over the pinned TBS instead), the IDNA row, rows for case-preserving IDNA, names UTS-46 refuses, an undecodable `xn--` label, `*`/`&` in the CN and an empty hostname, all regenerated from a Go 1.25 main module so crypto/x509 runs with the dcrd binary's GODEBUG defaults (the same generator with `x509sha256skid=0` reproduces the earlier dump byte for byte), and both error texts.  One known gap: the error text for an undecodable `xn--` label quotes the label with an approximation of Go's `%q` that keeps non-ASCII format, private-use and unassigned runes where Go writes `\u` escapes |

| daemon (`config`, `server`, `cmd/*`) | `dcroxide-node` | vectors | Complete: the configuration pipeline from `config.go` — the config struct with every option and dcrd's defaults, the four network parameter groupings with their RPC ports (`params.go`), and the full loadConfig validation and derivation gauntlet with dcrd's exact error strings: network selection, the acceptnonstd/rejectnonstd network-default resolution, per-network data/log dir namespacing, logsize parsing (all-digit values are invalid because the unit scan never breaks), the debug level grammar over the nineteen subsystem IDs with slog's level aliases, db type validation, utxo cache clamping, profile address validation (port-only values gain 127.0.0.1 and Go's SplitHostPort error texts surface), the ban/dial/idle duration floors with Go duration formatting in the errors, whitelist prefix parsing with dcrd 2.2's `netip` semantics (the `ParsePrefix`-then-`ParseAddr` chain with `Masked` normalization, zone acceptance-and-stripping on bare addresses, leading-zero prefix-length rejection, the `invalid whitelist value %q` error, and the family-strict `Contains` under which IPv4-mapped prefixes no longer match 4-byte candidates — pinned by a Go probe replicating master's block verbatim), addpeer/connect exclusivity with the listen/seeding side effects, default listeners, the RPC credential rules (basic-auth auto-disable, the localhost default listeners from an injected resolver, and the clientcert restrictions), localhost-only notls enforcement, minrelaytxfee amount conversion (`dcrutil.NewAmount` incl. the NaN rejection), block size bounds against the network maximum, index option conflicts, mining address decoding through the ported stdaddr with matching error texts, the mainnet unsynced-mining rejection with the simnet/regnet force-enable, listener/peer address normalization with interface expansion behind an injectable lookup, Tor/proxy wiring modeled as selection enums that the daemon's dial/lookup routing now realizes, the network interface states feeding getnetworkinfo, and TLS curve validation.  Also ported: `cleanAndExpandPath` over faithful ports of Go's `os.Expand` and `filepath.Clean` and the `~` handling (incl. the no-separator `~foo` quirk that joins onto the current user's home), `dcrutil.AppDataDir`, Go duration parsing/formatting, and `createDefaultConfigFile` over the embedded sample config with injected randomness.  The go-flags application-order semantics the pipeline observes are reproduced: the help pre-parse applies the command line before the config file, each parse pass resets slice options on first set — so command-line slice values wholly replace config file values while repeats within a pass accumulate — and regnet clears config-file peers only when the command line selected regnet.  Also ported: the go-flags v1.6.1 front-end over the 86-option registry (`load_config_from_argv`) — the command line scanner (long options with `=` or separate arguments, short options in separate/concatenated/`=` forms with cluster semantics, the negative-number exception for signed option types, quoted values through a `strconv.Unquote` port (every `UnquoteChar` escape including `\U`, hex digits by Go's `unhex`, and `\x`/octal escapes as raw bytes so escapes that spell UTF-8 build their character; a result that is not UTF-8, which Go keeps as raw bytes and a Rust `String` cannot hold, is refused with `the unquoted value is not valid UTF-8` — a deliberate divergence of the same kind as the argv and config-file UTF-8 refusals), `--` termination in the final parse only — so options after `--` leak through the help pre-parse into the effective config while still returning as remaining arguments, a pinned dcrd quirk — and go-flags' exact error texts: unknown flag, expected argument, got-option/got-double-dash, bool-flag-cannot-have-argument, and the `(expected TYPE)` marshal wrapper over Go's strconv/time `NumError` texts with base-0 integer parsing incl. hex/octal/underscores in `ParseUint`'s left-to-right order (an overflow ahead of a bad byte or a misplaced underscore is `value out of range`), `ParseFloat` through dcrjson's port (underscores, hex floats, no sign before `NaN`, `ErrRange` on overflow), and `ParseDuration`'s errors quoted with the time package's own `quote` (`\xHH` for non-ASCII and control bytes), its uint64 sum wrapping as Go's does); the INI grammar (trimmed `key=value` lines, `;`/`#` comments, quoted values, bool empty-value and `ParseBool` forms, bare keys as malformed-line errors, case-insensitive `[Application Options]` section resolution with unknown-group errors, and go-flags' name matching where the Go struct field name and even the single-character short name match ini keys while case variants of the long name do not, with per-line `file:line:` error prefixes; an empty key matches the first option, `ShowVersion`, through the empty `ini-name` tag; and go-flags' two passes — syntax errors anywhere in the file are reported first, then each section's values are looked up and converted in file order, so the first bad line of that walk is the error. Go walks the sections map in random order; the port walks sections in order of first appearance, one of the orders dcrd can take.); and the `env` tag defaults (`DCRD_APPDATA`, `DCRD_ALT_DNSNAMES` with comma splitting) applied at the end of each successful parse for options that parser never set — including the pinned quirk that an unset appdata takes the raw environment value after the home rewrite already derived the other paths from its cleaned form, and that config-file values suppress the environment while an explicit command line always wins.  Pinned by 76 pipeline scenarios plus 52 syntax scenarios from package-main dumps driving dcrd's real loadConfig over temp home directories: every effective config field, the cooked values (fee atoms, decoded mining addresses, whitelist networks via netip.Prefix.String, the three network info states, the selected params with its RPC port, and the per-subsystem log levels), the remaining arguments, and every error text — plus the sample config byte for byte, createDefaultConfigFile under both auth types with tokenized credentials, and a cleanAndExpandPath battery.  The help/version/debuglevel=show exits are represented as distinguished results for the daemon shell, and the help text itself is generated byte-for-byte as go-flags renders it (detailed in the daemon row, pinned by vectors from a checked-in generator over dcrd's verbatim config struct).  The OS error texts `loadConfig` prints are Go's `*PathError` forms (`gostd::GoPathError`, lower-case errno text).  They cover the missing or unreadable config file (`open <path>: ...`, `read <path>: is a directory`), the failed default config creation, and the home directory failure.  The home directory goes through a port of `os.MkdirAll` (`gostd::go_mkdir_all_owner_only`): it names the failing component, fails on the empty path an empty `--appdata` leaves, and keeps dcrd's dangling-symlink hint and its `errSuppressUsage` (no usage line).  Two approximations remain: a failure writing the default config after its open is labelled `open` rather than `write`, and off Unix the MkdirAll error names the whole path.  The parse-time stderr lines are written as dcrd reaches them, so they survive a later failure: the help pre-parse's go-flags `PrintErrors` echo, `Error creating a default config file`, and the deprecation and Tor-isolation notices.  The `dcrdLog` warnings (previous-testnet directories, missing config file) are logged by the daemon only after a successful load, so when `parseNetworkInterfaces` or `tlsCurve` then fails, a previous-testnet warning is lost where dcrd has already logged it.  Also ported: the pipe IPC lifecycle protocol (`ipc.go`) — the binary message framing (protocol version 1, type-length/type, little-endian payload length) for the `lifetimeevent`, `p2plistenaddr`, and `rpclistenaddr` messages Decrediton consumes over `--pipetx`, with every message kind pinned byte for byte against dcrd's own framing (the pipe reader/writer loops and the outgoing channel are daemon runtime); and `internal/version` — `parseSemVer` matching dcrd's semver regular expression exactly (hand-rolled with the numeric no-leading-zero rules and the pre-release/build identifier grammars) incl. the exact malformed-version and `strconv.ParseUint` range error texts, `NormalizeString`, and the pinned `2.2.0-pre` release version (master `452c1a6c`) with its parsed components, pinned by 83 verdict rows from an in-package dump over the upstream test table plus range and separator edge cases (dcrd's `vcsCommitID` build-info fallback does run at the pin — `2.2.0-pre` carries no build metadata (`internal/version/version.go:60`, `:166-168`), unlike `release-v2.1.5`'s `2.1.5+release.local` — and embeds the Go build info's VCS revision for a binary built from a git checkout; the port has no Go build info and deliberately models the module-build flavor with no commit suffix, which is what the test oracle links).  Also ported (server.go slice 1): the server's address bookkeeping — the bounded network address submission cache (`naSubmissionCache`: adds reset the score to one and stamp the injected clock, the oldest access time is evicted when the limit is reached even when re-adding an existing key, and per-type best submissions — dcrd breaks last-accessed and score ties by Go's random map iteration while iteration here is in key order, with the dump avoiding ties), `hostToNetAddress` over the address manager's host encoding with a resolver fallback (first resolved IP; "no addresses found" on empty), `ResolveLocalAddress` (the 60-percent-of-target-outbound majority ceiling, per-listener matching by exact host after IPv6 zone stripping, generic listeners, and probable-external endpoints by reachability class, adding manual-priority local addresses through the ported address manager with dcrd's abort-vs-skip error behavior), the wire/address-manager net address conversions, and the `hasServices`/v1-address-type-filter helpers.  Pinned by a scripted in-package session over a real address manager: cache states after adds, increments, re-adds, and eviction, the existence and error rows, per-type best submissions, six host conversions incl. resolver paths, and eight local-address resolution scenarios.  Also ported (server.go slice 2a): the serverPeer address relay and ban handler group — the per-peer known-address APBF (10000 items at a 0.001 false positive rate), the server-level addr push that filters known addresses, composes the ported peer push builder, and records the sent subset (dcrd carries an error-and-disconnect branch there, but the peer push never errors at the tag, so it is dead code — pinned by the empty-cache scenario keeping the peer connected), `addBanScore` over the ported dynamic ban score (banning disabled and whitelisted gating, the warn threshold at half the ban threshold, and the threshold-crossing ban), `BanPeer` (the banned-host map entry with the ban duration and the disconnect, with whitelist/disabled gating and the unsplittable-address disconnect-only fallback), `OnGetAddr` (simulation/regression network and outbound-peer fingerprinting gating, the once-per-connection flag, and the address-cache push), and `OnAddr` (the empty-list ban with dcrd's reason, the future-timestamp clamp to five days ago, per-address known marking, and the address manager forward; dcrd samples the connected flag per address against concurrent disconnects — the synchronous port samples it once).  Pinned by a scripted in-package session driving dcrd's real handlers on real serverPeer instances over piped connections (the in-tree peer module replaces the pinned one solely to enable the same self-connection bypass its own tests use; ping/pong round trips order the observations): the getaddr gating rows, the pushed-subset row (dcrd's address cache pick is random, so the row pins the count, cache membership, and the once-flag, and the exact pushed list feeds the replay's cache input), the known-address marks, the empty-list ban, the ban score ladder, and the BanPeer rows.  Also ported (server.go slice 2b): the abuse-control handler group — `OnMemPool` (the decaying 33-point flood score banning a fourth burst request, and the pool's transactions as queued inventory), `enforceNodeCFFlag` for the unsupported version 1 committed filter requests reached from `OnGetCFilter`/`OnGetCFHeaders`/`OnGetCFTypes` (at the negotiated protocol version a single violation scores exactly the ban threshold, which the strictly-greater rule does not cross, so the peer disconnects without a ban entry — pinned; with banning disabled only the disconnect remains), and `OnNotFound` (the block/transaction/mix inventory classification with the invalid-type disconnect, the 20-per-block persistent and 10-per-item transient scores with dcrd's pluralized reason strings, and the sync manager forward, which is ported netsync machinery) with `pickNoun`.  Pinned by scripted sessions over the same piped-peer rig: the four-request mempool ladder, both filter enforcement variants, and the four notfound classifications, each observing the banned-host map and connection state.  Also ported (server.go slice 2c): the version handshake handlers — `OnVersion` (the address manager service-flag update for outbound peers, skipped for inbound peers and on the simulation and regression networks and performed before any rejection; the disconnect for protocol versions below the reject-removal version; the mix-capable outbound maintenance check — evaluated between the two rejections, wanting three mix-capable outbound peers clamped to the outbound target and disconnecting a pre-mix-version outbound peer when the remaining slots are needed, deliberately falling through without returning so the address manager work below still runs and the peer is still marked good, pinned; the disconnect for outbound peers missing the network service flag; and the outbound non-simulation tail: advertising the best local address only when listening and the sync manager reports current — a branch the dump cannot reach without a live sync manager, so it is exercised natively over the slice 1 pieces — requesting more addresses when the address manager wants them, and marking the peer's address good) with the relay-transaction preference taken from the message's disable-relay flag, and the post-handshake sendheaders (dcrd `serverPeer.Run` queueing it before `AddPeer`; the pin has no `OnVerAck`, and the daemon queues it in `peerloop`).  Pinned by five native tests over the 2.2 handler: the services update that precedes every rejection, the old-protocol rejection, the mix-capable outbound preference that now rejects where pre-2.2 it disconnected and deliberately continued, the missing-services rejection, the accept paths, and dcrd's exact rejection texts.  The dump that pinned the pre-2.2 handler was retired with it in 940d6e4: the address advertisement, getaddr request and good marking its scenarios covered moved out of `OnVersion` into post-handshake add-peer admission, so the fall-through those rows exercised no longer exists.  Also ported (server.go slice 3, since re-ported to the pin): the peer state maps and admission, as decision models the daemon does not call — `peerState` (the inbound/outbound/persistent peer maps and the banned-host map with ban-lift times) with its count helper; `handleAddPeer` (the shutdown rejection, then `considerReportedAddr` over the server's external-address candidate cache ahead of the map placement; the pin checks bans before the handshake in `handleBannedConn` and enforces the single-IP and max-peer limits in the connection manager, so the model no longer takes release-v2.1.5's `sp.Addr()`, whitelist and `MaxSameIP` inputs); and `DonePeer` (a tracked peer is dropped from its map and nothing else happens; an untracked peer's address-manager connection time is refreshed, skipped on the simulation networks, with neither the version/verack gate nor the connection-manager release that release-v2.1.5 had) — and `isWhitelisted` (dcrd `connmgr.IsWhitelisted`: `netip.Prefix.Contains` over the masked whitelist prefixes, so a candidate of the other address family never matches), which the daemon's accept path does call and which reads the candidate IP in the `To4`-canonical form the address manager stores it in, without the IPv6 zone that dcrd's `NetAddress` never carries. `BanPeer`'s host is zone-free in the same way, so link-local peers are banned and whitelisted as in dcrd (pinned by `tests/review_ban.rs`). The daemon keeps no server peer maps. Registration and teardown run in `dispatch::ServerPeerHandler::on_connected`/`on_disconnected` (the outbound address-manager block, `considerReportedAddr`, and the sync manager's `OnPeerConnected`/`OnPeerDisconnected`); the ban gate runs in `runtime` over `ServerContext::banned_hosts`; the connection limits live in `dcroxide-connmgr`. Teardown never refreshes the address manager, which is the pin's tracked-peer path. dcrd reaches the untracked path only for a peer whose `handleAddPeer` was refused because the server was shutting down, and the daemon has no such refusal. Pinned by the thirteen release-v2.1.5 dump rows whose behaviour the pin kept, replayed against the models (`srvadmit_vectors.rs`): the ban gate and expiry, outbound and persistent placement, the tracked removal, the untracked-peer timestamp refresh with its simnet skip, and six whitelist rows (which the pin's netip check reproduces). The external-address scenarios moved to `srvextaddr_vectors.rs` (dumped from 036b7090). `connectionsWithIP` and `disconnectPeer`, which the pin no longer has, were dropped from the port along with the dump's disconnect-peer rows.  Also ported (server.go slice 4): the relay and broadcast decision core — `handleRelayPeerInvMsg` (the not-connected and required-services gates, the block-announcement de-duplication that clears the peer's last announced block when the same block arrives twice and records it otherwise, the headers-versus-inventory preference where a wanted-headers peer receives a headers message built from the announcement's block header and drops the announcement when the data is not a header, the transaction path that gates on the peer's relay-disabled flag and the data being a transaction before tracking it as recently advertised, the mix path that gates on relay-disabled and the negotiated protocol version being at least the mix version, and the immediate-versus-batched queue selection) and the per-peer body of `handleBroadcastMsg` (connected and not in the exclusion set).  Pinned by fifteen scenarios from a dump driving dcrd's real handlers on live piped serverPeers and observing the announced-block field, the recently advertised transaction cache, and the peer-visible inv, headers, and message deliveries (the batched inventory is observed within the 100-to-500ms trickle window): the two gates, the de-duplication, the inventory and headers block-announcement paths with the bad-data drop, the transaction relay with its disabled and bad-data variants, the three mix outcomes, immediate relay, and the broadcast delivery and exclusion.  Also ported (server.go slice 5): the inv and getdata intake gates — `OnGetData` up to the serve queue (the empty-request ban with dcrd's reason; the decaying request ban score that scores an oversized inventory query at ninety-nine hundredths of a full message, so one full query warns without banning while a second crosses the threshold and dcrd bans with the "ban score exceeds threshold" reason; the max-concurrent-request-batch and max-pending-item disconnects; and the enqueue that advances the pending item count) and `OnInv`'s classification (the empty-announcement ban with dcrd's reason, and in blocks-only mode the transaction and mix announcement disconnects with dcrd's nouns, otherwise a forward to the ported sync manager); the daemon-side known-inventory record happens only on that forward path and only for the block, transaction and mixing types dcrd's netsync switch handles (`internal/netsync/manager.go:1908`, `:1917`, `:1942` — the switch has no default arm, so an error, filtered-block or unknown vector is forwarded without ever entering the thousand-entry set), taking the peer registry lock once per announcement rather than once per vector while leaving the relay lock per put as dcrd's `AddKnownInventory` does.  Two residual divergences on that path: the port keeps a second known-inventory set on the daemon side where dcrd keeps one (netsync's `Peer` embeds `*peer.Peer`, `internal/netsync/manager.go:110`), and the daemon-side record is applied ahead of netsync's shutdown and peer-presence gates (`internal/netsync/manager.go:1880`) rather than behind them.  Pinned by nine scenarios from a dump driving dcrd's real handlers on live piped serverPeers and reading the banned-host map, the connection state, and the pending getdata item counter directly: the empty ban, the small enqueue, the warn-then-ban score ladder over two full queries, the two capacity disconnects, and the inv empty ban with both blocks-only disconnects (the forward outcome is netsync machinery not reachable from the dump, exercised natively).  Also ported (server.go, chain-backed): `OnGetBlocks` and `OnGetHeaders` response construction — the getblocks inventory build over the ported `LocateBlocks` output (skipping inventory the peer is already known to have, and storing the continue hash when the response fills an entire five-hundred-vector message so the peer's request for the final block triggers the next batch), and the getheaders decision over the ported `LocateHeaders` and `ChainWork` outputs (an empty headers message when the local best known tip's cumulative work — compared against the minimum known work by the ported uint256 ordering — is too little, otherwise the located headers, with a chain-work lookup error skipping the empty-response gate).  The chain query walks themselves are the ported blockchain engine's, pinned separately by its own vectors; these pin the server-specific wrapping.  Pinned by seven scenarios from a dump that built a real regnet chain from dcrd's own fullblocktests generator (twenty-five accepted blocks processed through `blockchain.ProcessBlock`) and drove dcrd's real handlers over a live piped serverPeer, freezing the located hashes/headers as row inputs and observing the peer-visible inv and headers deliveries: the genesis-locator, stop-hash, at-tip (empty), and known-inventory-filtered getblocks rows, and the normal, at-tip, and low-work getheaders rows; the continue-hash-on-full-message branch is unreachable below five hundred blocks and is pinned directly (the block hashes differ per generation because the generator stamps wall-clock timestamps, so each row is self-contained).  Also ported (server.go, chain-backed): `handleServeGetData` — the getdata serve queue as a decision core over the per-inventory fetch resolutions (a transaction from the recently advertised cache or mempool, a block from the chain, a mix message from the mix pool): each resolved data message is queued in request order, the misses are accumulated into a single consolidated notfound message sent last, a served block that was the advertised continuation triggers a best-tip inventory message afterward and clears the stored continuation hash, an unknown inventory type is skipped entirely (neither served, recorded as not found, nor counted against the pending decrements), and served and not-found items each decrement the pending item counter; dcrd's `maxPendingSend` send semaphore is reproduced by `SendPipeline`. It infers completed writes from the peer's sent-byte counter, because the port's output loop carries no per-message done channel. Traffic other producers write while payloads are outstanding can retire a slot early, but credit beyond everything queued is discarded rather than banked against later payloads, so the bound still holds on a long-lived connection (pinned by `earlier_traffic_does_not_pre_pay_later_payloads`). The item fetches are the caller's seams.  Pinned by eight scenarios from a dump that drove dcrd's real handleServeGetData over a real regnet chain (built from the fullblocktests generator) with empty mempool and mix pools, recording the ordered sequence of messages the remote received: the multi-block serve, the block/transaction/mix not-found cases, the unknown-type skip, a mixed batch interleaving served blocks with a miss, and the continuation-hash block that appends the best-tip inventory and clears the hash.  Also ported (server.go, chain/mempool-backed): `OnGetInitState` — the init state response assembly as a decision core over the requested types and the `SortParentsByVotes`/`VoteHashesForBlock`/`TSpendHashes` seam outputs: the once-per-connection guard, the empty response before stake validation height, fetching the head blocks (capped at eight) when either they or their votes are requested, extending the votes per capped block in order, passing through the treasury spends, clearing the head blocks when only votes were requested, and dropping the response as a build error when a wire list limit is exceeded.  Pinned by seven scenarios from a dump driving dcrd's real OnGetInitState over real regnet chains built from the fullblocktests generator (one past the stake validation height, one below it) with an empty mempool: the below-stake blank push, the once-flag second-request suppression, and the type combinations — the empty mempool leaves the seams empty so the dump pins the once-flag, the blank gate, and the past-stake empty push, while the block cap, the vote extension, the clear-when-only-votes rule, and the wire limits are pinned directly with crafted facts (a voting mempool is not cheaply seedable in the dump).  Also ported: the `dcroxide` daemon binary — the runtime front-end of `dcrd.go`'s `dcrdMain`: it builds the configuration environment from the real operating system (the application data directory with Go's GOOS names, the localhost resolver, and the system random source.  The current user's home for a leading `~` resolves as dcrd's `user.Current` does on every unix but Apple's: the cgo-free lookup of the password-file entry for the real uid, falling back to `$HOME` only when there is none and `$USER` is set.  On macOS and Windows std's `home_dir` stands in.  It takes `$HOME` (macOS) or a non-empty `%USERPROFILE%` (Windows) first, where Go reads only the directory-service entry (`getpwuid_r`) or the process token's profile directory (`GetUserProfileDirectory`), so the two differ when that variable points away from the account's own directory.  On Windows `\` as well as `/` ends a `~user` name.  Interface-name and cross-user home resolution are not yet wired), parses the command line through the ported configuration pipeline, handles the help, version, and `--debuglevel=show` exits with dcrd's exit codes (the show exit is now surfaced as a distinguished result mirroring dcrd's `os.Exit(0)` right before the debug level parse), prints the startup banner, opens (or creates) the block database at `<datadir>/blocks_<dbtype>` and initializes the chain state through the ported chain engine (a fresh database creates the genesis chain state, an existing one is reopened), announces the loaded best block height and hash, creates the address manager and loads the persisted `peers.json`, announces the loaded address count and the configured peer-to-peer listeners and seeding, binds the peer-to-peer listeners and serves inbound peers through the threaded listener runtime (building the peer template with `PeerTemplate::from_config`, which takes dcrd `newPeerConfig`'s configuration-driven fields: the network, `SFNodeNetwork`, the user agent, `--peeridletimeout` as the idle timeout beside dcrd's constant ping interval, `--blocksonly` as the version message's `DisableRelayTx`, and `--proxy` for hiding a proxy-host connection's `addr_you`; then starting the runtime with the inbound-peer handler), and idles on a SIGINT/SIGTERM shutdown listener until interrupted, at which point it disconnects the live peers and stops accepting new connections.  Served peers' messages now flow through the server-handler dispatch: a per-peer handler (the message-handling slice of dcrd's `serverPeer`) over a shared `ServerContext` (the chain behind a mutex plus the network's minimum known chain work and the banning configuration) answers `getheaders` through the ported low-work gate and `LocateHeaders`, `getblocks` through `LocateBlocks` with the known-inventory skip and the continue-hash bookkeeping, and `getdata` through dcrd's intake gates (empty-request ban, the decaying oversized-request ban score with the whitelist exemption, the request limits) and the serve queue — blocks fetched from the chain, transaction/mix requests answered in the consolidated notfound exactly like empty pools, unknown inventory types skipped, and the continuation triggering the best-tip inventory (the ban outcomes currently drop the connection; the ban-list bookkeeping refusing reconnects followed with the peer-state wiring, covered below; dcrd's asynchronous serve queue with its semaphore is served inline on the input thread, so batches never overlap by construction).  The stake ticket treap's structural sharing moved from `Rc` to `Arc` so the chain crosses into the peer threads (result-invariant; the dcrd treap battery pins the shape).  The addr exchange is also dispatched: getaddr answers an inbound peer once per connection with the randomized address-cache subset through the ported pushAddrMsg (known-address filtering and tracking), and addr intake clamps far-future timestamps, tracks the addresses as known to the peer, and feeds the shared address manager, with an empty list dropping the connection.  The committed-filter and init-state handlers are also dispatched: getcfilterv2 serves a block's version 2 filter with its header commitment inclusion proof and getcfsv2 the batched ancestry range (both silently ignoring unknown blocks and missing filters exactly like dcrd), and getinitstate answers once per connection — the empty message before stake validation, and the filled response past it (whose eligible head blocks, votes, and treasury spends await the mempool and tip-generation wiring; the daemon cannot sync past stake validation before the sync manager lands).  Inventory announcements pass the ported intake gate (an empty announcement drops the connection, and blocks-only mode disconnects peers announcing transactions or mix messages), with the passing announcements' forward awaiting the sync-manager driver.  The netsync seams are in place: a chain adapter hands the sync manager the shared chain (injecting the server's median-adjusted time where dcrd reads its median-time source), the not-yet-wired mempool and mixpool stand in as empty pools that reject everything, and the daemon constructs the manager with dcrd's config surface (the params-derived minimum work and target block time, the recently-confirmed APBF at dcrd's 23000/1e-6 sizing, and targetOutbound capped by --maxpeers) — the chain gained dcrd's exported BestHeader/HaveHeader/HaveBlock/IsCurrent/MaybeUpdateIsCurrent wrappers for it.  The sync manager is now driven: each handshaken peer is registered with it through the serve lifecycle hooks (dcrd `AddPeer`/`DonePeer` signalling `OnPeerConnected`/`OnPeerDisconnected`), a peer-id registry resolves the manager's actions to any live peer's outbound queue or socket (queue-message and disconnect actions; the stall-timer actions await the timer piece), and the headers/block/transaction intake plus the passing inventory announcements flow through the manager's decision cores — so a data-serving peer connecting to a stale chain receives the daemon's getheaders immediately after the handshake, the beginning of the initial header sync.  The header-sync stall watchdog runs as its own timer thread: the manager's reset/stop actions arm and disarm it, and when the armed timeout elapses the manager's stall handler disconnects the unresponsive sync peer (dcrd's stallHandler timer case, at dcrd's timeout with the duration injected for tests).  The connection manager drives outbound dials: an event-loop driver over the ported decision-core `ConnManager` opens the permanent `--connect` connections, carries each dialed socket into the shared serve path (so a dialed peer runs the same `serverPeer` dispatch and registers with the sync manager, the direction that actually syncs the daemon), and keeps them up with the manager's retry backoff — the daemon now seeks out peers rather than only accepting them.  The address-manager automatic dialing now feeds the same driver: the ported `newAddressFunc` draws candidates from the address manager, skipping a group the daemon already has an outbound connection to (tracked as dcrd's `outboundGroups`), recently attempted addresses, and non-default ports on early tries, and the manager fills its target-outbound slots from that source when no `--connect` peers are set; a dialed peer's version exchange marks the address good, requests more addresses when the manager is low, and advertises the best local address to a current, listening node (dcrd `OnVersion`'s outbound branch).  The HTTPS seeders bootstrap the address manager: each configured seeder is queried on its own thread through a TLS transport (`ureq` over rustls — the workspace's first TLS dependency, chosen with the future TLS RPC server in mind) with dcrd's one-minute timeout, the ported response handling lands the discovered addresses with the seeder's resolved IP as their source, and a fully failed round retries on dcrd's one-to-ten-second backoff while the manager still needs addresses.  The RPC server now answers over HTTP: a per-connection threaded listener parses requests with dcrd's authenticated read limit, authenticates through the ported `checkAuth` (dcrd's 401 with its realm on failure), and runs bodies through the ported `jsonRPCRead` pipeline over a chain adapter (a growing slice of the handler seam trait — the chain adapter now delegates the block/header/height/median-time/live-ticket/ticket-pool queries straight to the chain, a connection-manager adapter answers the connected count over the live-peer registry, and a sync-manager adapter answers the sync height and peer id, so getbestblockhash/getblockcount/getblockhash/getblock/getbestblock/getconnectioncount and their kin answer over the running daemon; getinfo, getblockchaininfo, and getnettotals answer too — the chain gained dcrd's exported MaxBlockSize (the max-block-size vote over the branch view), the threshold-state tuple converts onto the RPC agenda states (the winning choice dropped exactly as dcrd's status mapping does), the time source reports the offset of the ported median time source (`mediantime.rs`, dcrd `blockchain.MedianTimeSource`, which `OnVersion` feeds after its rejections, one sample per source address, with the 200-entry cap, the median taken only over an odd count of at least five samples, and the 70-minute clamp with its invalid-clock warning — pinned by `median_time_matches_dcrd` and `version_timestamps_feed_the_median_time_source` — and which every adjusted-time seam reads: the sync adapter, the chain-notification drain, the template generator for its template timestamps and its is-current gate (pinned by `the_generator_gate_reads_the_median_adjusted_time`), the RPC sanity checker and the RPC is-current check), the system clock backs dcrd's rpcClock, and every peer transport feeds a server-wide received/sent byte-totals pair from the handshake onward (dcrd's OnRead/OnWrite accounting; the port counts a message's bytes on successful decode, so a rejected oversized header contributes nothing where dcrd would count its 24 header bytes — noted divergence; the read listener's ban half runs on the handshake reads too, as dcrd's does with no handshake-state guard, and only for failures carrying a dcrd `wire.ErrorCode` — a body that ends mid-decode is Go's `io.ErrUnexpectedEOF` in dcrd, which `errors.As` does not match, so both disconnect without banning); a handler touching a still-unwired daemon seam (the mempool, mining, and indexer subsystems) answers an internal error and the server survives); the RPC machinery's boxed subsystem seams gained `Send` bounds so the server crosses the connection threads.  dcrd's default TLS mode is served: the daemon loads or generates the `rpc.cert`/`rpc.key` pair exactly as `genCertPair` does (the ported certgen's pair on the `--tlscurve` curve (P-256 by default, P-521 the alternative) with dcrd's ten-year validity and autogenerated organization, the key written owner-only) and wraps each RPC connection in a rustls server session — noting that dcrd's autogenerated certificate is a self-signed CA served directly as the end-entity certificate, which Go clients accept but webpki-based clients must pin exactly as Decred tooling pins rpc.cert (the end-to-end check does the same).  The websocket endpoint is served too: a `GET /ws` request is authenticated (allowing an unauthenticated upgrade that must authenticate in-band), validated and answered with the RFC 6455 handshake (the accept key is the SHA-1-and-base64 of the client key and the protocol GUID), and then each JSON-RPC request arrives as a masked text frame through a from-scratch RFC 6455 server frame transport (fragment reassembly, ping/pong, the cumulative read limit, protocol-error close frames) and runs dcrd's `inHandler` gate — an unauthenticated client must `authenticate` first, limited users are refused non-limited methods, null-id notifications draw no reply — before dispatching through the ported websocket service handler; the subscription commands are recorded by a notification manager that now also DELIVERS: a delivery thread (dcrd's `notificationHandler` goroutine in threaded form) receives chain and mempool events over a channel, runs the ported per-event builders against the subscribed clients (block connected/disconnected with the per-client filtered transaction list, work, tspend, reorganization, winning tickets, new tickets, mempool acceptance with the relevant-transaction filter pass over every client, and mix messages), and queues the marshalled JSON on each client's outbound queue, which the serving loop drains whenever the connection goes idle (the read timeout doubles as the notification poll, replacing the 30-second connection timeout that would otherwise kill idle websocket clients dcrd keeps alive indefinitely); a disconnecting client is removed from the registry and every subscription map except mix messages, exactly the map dcrd's unregister-client case skips.  The frame transport follows gorilla v1.5.1's `advanceFrame`.  Everything the two header bytes show is judged first, and all of it is reported in one 1002 close with gorilla's reason texts.  The read limit is enforced on the message's running total of declared lengths before a payload is read, and the 1009 carries no reason.  A declared length negative as an `int64` fails with no close frame.  A received close is checked against `isValidReceivedCloseCode` and for UTF-8, and is echoed as its code alone, or empty when it carried none.  Requests are read from their raw bytes on both transports, as dcrd hands them to `json.Unmarshal`.  Invalid UTF-8 inside a string is served and decoded as U+FFFD, one per byte (`utf8.DecodeRune`).  A stray byte elsewhere is Go's syntax error.  Scanner messages name a byte as Go 1.26's `quoteChar` does (`'ÿ'`, `'\u0080'`, `'\a'`).  That is the `encoding/json` v1 of the toolchain in dcrd's release image (`contrib/docker/Dockerfile`, golang:1.26.2).  Go 1.27 as installed on the dev machine (go1.27.1) builds `encoding/json` on its v2 implementation and spells a high byte `'\xff'`.  `tools/oracle` builds with whatever `go` is on the path, so an oracle built with 1.27 disagrees with dcrd's release build on JSON error text.  The chain now emits dcrd's `blockchain.Notification` events through an installable synchronous callback: the early new-tip check (only while current, before the expensive connect), acceptance after any resulting reorganization (with dcrd's quirk of sending the processed block for every node an out-of-order delivery links, and the post-reorg best height), connected and disconnected blocks with their active agenda flags, new tickets at and above the stake enabled height with the next stake difficulty, and the reorg-started / reorganization / reorg-done sequence for competing-branch reorganizations (never for plain extensions; done fires even when every attempt failed, like dcrd's deferred send).  The callback runs inside the chain's critical section — dcrd releases its chain lock around some sends where the daemon holds the mutex throughout — so apart from the early checked-block relay it only queues.  That relay is dcrd's own under its held chain lock, and the peer registry and per-peer relay locks it takes never wait on the chain mutex.  The daemon-side handler (dcrd `server.handleBlockchainNotification`) is installed as the chain's callback at startup: connected and disconnected blocks, reorganizations, and new tickets queue for the post-processing drain in the chain's emission order and reach the websocket notification manager's queue from there.  A client therefore sees txaccepted before blockconnected, blockconnected before newtickets, and a reorg's blockdisconnected/blockconnected frames before reorganization, as dcrd's single notification queue delivers them.  The one single-client exception: the background template generator is still fed at callback time, so a client subscribed to both notifyblocks and notifywork can receive a work notification ahead of the blockconnected frame dcrd queues first.  An accepted block runs dcrd's winning-tickets gate (stake validation at hand, reorg depth under six, the old-mainnet pre-vote-version skip, and the never-pruned broadcast dedup set of the reference release); because the callback runs under the chain mutex, the gate-passing blocks queue their lottery lookups and the sync adapter drains them right after each processing call returns — the same released-lock situation dcrd's inline lookup runs under — with the unsynced-mining/is-current gate evaluated at the drain.  The reorg-started/done events now reach the background template generator, and the mix-observer refusal gate runs over the live mixpool — both covered below.  The early new-tip announcement relays straight from the `NTNewTipBlockChecked` callback, under the chain mutex, as dcrd relays it with its chain lock held.  The block therefore spreads while the connect runs, and a checked block that later fails to connect has still been announced, like dcrd's.  This replaces an earlier post-processing drain that gave up dcrd's early-relay latency win.  The UTXO set now backs the RPC seams too: `gettxout` resolves entries through `Chain::fetch_utxo_entry` over the loaded set (falling through an empty-mempool `FetchTransaction` stub that answers dcrd's transaction-is-not-in-the-pool error, discarded like dcrd's handler does), `gettxoutsetinfo` runs a ported `FetchStats` (a forced cache flush that also writes the utxo set state, then a fold over the set in serialized-key byte order — including dcrd's VLQ quirk where output index 16512 sorts before 16511 — producing the counts, distinct-transaction tally, serialized size, total atoms, and the merkle root of the BLAKE-256 entry hashes), and `createrawssrtx`'s ticket lookup decodes the entry's stored minimal outputs through the ported `deserializeToMinimalOutputs`; the treasury and auto-revocations agenda-active queries answer over the chain's deployment state with dcrd's zero-hash special case.  The transaction index now runs in the daemon (dcrd `newServer`'s index block): startup always drops the legacy address index then honors `--droptxindex`/`--dropexistsaddrindex` by dropping, closing the database (dcrd's deferred `db.Close()`, whose flush is what makes the drop durable; before the review of 2026-09-23 the port exited without it and a small drop never left the metadata overlay), and exiting, then drops the legacy v1 committed filter index, exactly in dcrd's order between `loadBlockDB` and `newServer`; with `--txindex` the daemon creates the `IndexSubscriber` and `TxIndex` over a `ChainQueryer` adapter locking the shared chain (the database handle is cloneable now, sharing the one open database between the chain and the indexes like copies of dcrd's `database.DB` interface value) and synchronously catches the index up to the main chain (recovery of an off-main-chain tip included); each drained connect/disconnect block event then notifies the subscriber at the end of its case with the block, parent, and treasury flag — dcrd's buffered-channel handler goroutine delivered synchronously with identical state transitions. A failed update is logged (dcrd's `log.Error(err)` under INDX) and ends that notification's walk over the subscriptions. Later notifications are still processed, as dcrd's handler loop processes them (its `s.cancel()` cancels only the context the indexes were initialized with). An index that missed an update therefore logs the missing-notification error for each later block and resumes once the heights line up again; `getrawtransaction` serves over the live index through the ported handler's seams — the index name/tip/entry lookups, the sync wait polling dcrd's `maybeNotifySubscribers` condition against the three-second `syncWait` (dcrd's 500ms ticker traded for a poll of the same condition), and the block-region fetch straight off the shared database — and getinfo reports the enabled index.  Pinned by seam unit tests over a genesis chain plus an end-to-end check that builds a regnet chain from dcrd's own full-block battery, catches the index up over processed history, connects the next battery block through the live callback-drain path, and serves the coinbase over HTTP: the non-verbose hex byte-for-byte from the stored block region, the verbose block hash/height/confirmations with dcrd's `omitempty` quirk dropping the coinbase's zero `blockindex`, the no-information error, and getinfo's txindex flag.  The exists address index runs alongside it (dcrd `newServer` creating `NewExistsAddrIndex` after the txindex unless `--noexistsaddrindex`, both sharing the one subscriber and the one synchronous catch-up): the pool records every added unconfirmed transaction's addresses through the mempool's exists-addr hook, and `existsaddress`/`existsaddresses` serve over the live index through the same Tip/+5/sync-wait gauntlet with dcrd's LSB-first bitset hex for the batch form.  The chain notification handler now builds unconditionally with an optional websocket manager (dcrd's nil `rpcServer` checks gate only the ws sends), so the index maintenance and the mempool block-connect/disconnect maintenance run under `--norpc` too — closing the gap noted with the txindex piece; the winning-tickets pipeline keeps dcrd's `rpcServer != nil` first condition (no manager → no lottery lookups and no broadcast-set growth), and the `existsaddress` sync wait shares the noted `getrawtransaction` global-mutex stall divergence.  The rebroadcast machinery runs too (dcrd `rebroadcastHandler`): a dedicated thread owns the pending inventory fed add/remove/prune commands over a channel exactly like dcrd's `modifyRebroadcastInv`, waits dcrd's fixed five minutes before the first resend and a uniformly random duration up to thirty minutes between the rest (rejection-sampled without modulo bias like dcrd's `crypto/rand.Duration`), re-relays every pending entry through the recently-advertised cache with `immediate` false, and prunes on dcrd's three rules — a ticket priced off the next stake difficulty, an expired ticket, and a revocation whose ticket is not in the live set (dcrd's check is strict live-treap membership, so a normally-missed ticket's pending revocation is pruned on the first pass; kept bug for bug).  `sendrawtransaction` now feeds it — the connection-manager seam's `add_rebroadcast_inventory` was an unimplemented stub that would have panicked the RPC thread on any live non-vote submission — and every connected block's non-coinbase transactions run dcrd's `TransactionConfirmed`: the recently-confirmed filter (now an `Arc<Mutex<…>>` shared between the netsync manager and the chain handler, exactly as dcrd shares the server's filter with its netsync config) records the hash unconditionally while the rebroadcast removal and the block-change prunes keep dcrd's `rpcServer != nil` gates.  Pinned by unit tests driving the prune rules over a real battery chain with real dcrd-generated stake transactions, the timer thread firing into the advertised cache, the unbiased interval bounds, and an end-to-end drain check that a connected block's regular and stake transactions land in the shared filter while the coinbase never does.  New blocks announce to the served peers (dcrd's two `RelayBlockAnnouncement` passes): the early checked announcement relays straight from the `NTNewTipBlockChecked` callback to full-node peers only (the chain gates its emission on being current), under the chain mutex exactly where dcrd relays it with its chain lock held, so it leaves before the connect and its commit (the peer registry and per-peer relay locks it takes never wait on the chain mutex), the accepted announcement queues for every peer with dcrd's sync gate applied at drain time (not relayed unless current or unsynced mining is allowed — dcrd's `SyncManager.IsCurrent`, read through `SyncGate` over the sync manager's shared is-current flag and sync height, as with the winning-tickets drain), and the per-peer fan-out mirrors `handleRelayPeerInvMsg`'s block branch exactly: the announced-block toggle suppresses the duplicate second pass, peers that sent `sendheaders` (a new dispatch arm flips the registered preference, dcrd's `sendHeadersPreferred`) receive the header itself as a headers message bypassing the known-inventory set, and the rest get the immediate inventory.  Pinned by a fan-out unit test over three peers (services gate, headers preference, the toggle across the checked/accepted passes, and the known-inventory dedup on a third pass — dcrd's toggle semantics kept bug for bug), a chain-driven unit test that finds the announcement already queued when the chain emits the event, before the block connects, a drain unit test of the sync gate over a stale chain, and an end-to-end check where a served peer over a real connection receives a connected battery block as an inventory and, after sendheaders, the next one as the raw header.  The block template builder now runs over the live chain: the chain gained dcrd's exported `CalcStakeVersionByHash`, `CalcNextRequiredDifficulty`-by-hash, `IsHeaderCommitmentsAgendaActive`, and `FetchUtxoViewParentTemplate` (the headercmt.go port — the tip block's spend journal disconnects the tip onto the parent's point of view, then the candidate template connects on top; the parent's own journal feeds the disapproval path the port's view-connect signature takes where dcrd loads it internally), and the node crate's mining adapters stand in for dcrd's mining config closures: `NodeTemplateChain` answers all twenty-five template-chain queries over the shared chain (transaction-input checks resolve from the passed view exactly like dcrd's closure, the no-regular-transactions view reuses the mempool adapter's disconnect-disapproved-tip recipe and its per-tip memo (one per template build), and the standard verify flags recipe is shared) while `NodeTemplateTxSource` forwards the mining view and vote queries through the pool mutex.  Pinned by an end-to-end check that builds a consensus-valid template over a live regnet battery chain with the real pool — the builder's internal `CheckConnectBlockTemplate` runs against the real chain — paying a real mining address; the byte-exact template vectors are unchanged.  The generator now runs in the daemon (dcrd `newServer` launching `bgblktmplgenerator` when mining addresses are configured): a dedicated thread drives the ported synchronous state machine, reconstructing dcrd's four timers (the 30/1-second regen, the 2.5-second max-votes propagation, the 3-second side-chain tracking, and the 1-second failed-generation retry) as absolute deadlines in a receive-with-timeout loop; each build executes the latest queued request through the live template builder.  When it returns, the commands and timers that came due during the build are serviced through the state machine before the result is installed, as dcrd's regen handler services them concurrently with the generation goroutine.  A build superseded by a new request (or a shutdown) is then dropped without installing or notifying, like dcrd's cancelled goroutine, and the newest request is built.  Otherwise the result is fed back through the state machine (the template-update event syncs the base block and re-arms the regen timer) and out to the getwork subscribers.  The one remaining difference is timing: dcrd runs a cancelled build concurrently with its replacement, while the thread finishes it first.  Template retrieval (getwork's current-template read, template subscriptions, the CPU miner) waits while the state machine's stale-template count is nonzero, which means a new-parent or new-votes build is in flight or a reorganization is unfinished; dcrd's `currentTemplate` waits on `staleTemplateWg` the same way.  The wait additionally ends when the RPC request is cancelled or the generator thread exits; dcrd's `Wait` has neither exit.
